# The Remnant of Promise Official Study Bible — Master Roadmap

This is the single source of truth for the build of the Remnant of Promise Official Study Bible app. Standing rules, locked decisions, open questions, the phase plan, file structure, and the connection to the larger mission all live in this one file. Edit this file in place — do not create dated duplicates or stacked versions. Per the two-file convention from the yoshi-voice skill, this roadmap and the running app folder are the project's interface; everything else is supporting structure underneath.

---

## I. What This Is

A study Bible app published by Remnant of Promise (More Than Milk Ministries imprint for any printed companion volumes), built to fund the assembly's mission while serving the awakening remnant with a Bible that does what every other Bible app refuses to do: restore the sacred names with parenthetical equivalents on every mention, restore *son of Adam* wherever the translators flattened it to *son of man*, present the canon and the extra-canonical witnesses without the inherited Christian filter, and surface Yoshi's framework reading inline as commentary the reader can lean on while they walk through the text.

The aim, plainly: a Logos-class study tool for the awakening reader, at $0 entry, with everything that matters faithful to what Yahuah (God) actually said.

The funding plan it slots into is the Mission Statement's: ten thousand partners contributing one hundred dollars a month, twenty-five percent funding the assembly itself, seventy-five percent going back out to the hungry, the homeless, the widow's broken roof, free home repair, the destitute member who needs an interest-free loan up to ten thousand dollars on Deuteronomy 23:19 terms. The app is one of the channels that gathers the ten thousand. It is not the work; it serves the work.

---

## II. What's Already Built

Substantial. Before any line of code is written, this project starts with these existing assets — all already on Yoshi's Desktop, all already through Yoshi's voice gate:

**Restored sacred-name editions, published.** The Apocrypha (Restored Names Edition), the Book of Jasher (Restored Names Edition), the Book of Enoch (Restored Names Edition), and the Book of Jubilees (Restored Names Edition) are already published on KDP and are working source text for the extras tier. They have been through the voice-skill rules; they will be the byte-equivalence target the deterministic restoration pipeline (Phase 3) gets validated against.

**The Teaching Corpus.** Forty framework concepts mapped (`~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/concept-list.md`), seventeen published books indexed against those concepts, thirteen teaching videos catalogued (one transcript saved, the rest pending). Each concept folder has the per-concept file structure already designed: `summary.md`, `from-books.md`, `from-videos.md`, `verses.md`, `yoshi-clarifications.md`, `open-questions.md`. This is the upstream that projects into the in-app commentary. The Teaching Corpus README explicitly names "Bible app commentary" as one of the downstream projections — Yoshi designed it for this exactly.

**The Statement of Faith.** Twenty-three sections published at `remnantofpromise.org/statement-of-faith/` — eight short doorway sections (the gospel held plainly) plus fifteen deeper-dive sections (the diagnostic and the framework). This is the app's onboarding flow before the reader ever opens Genesis. Section §XI alone (*The Judaizer Error Across the Ages*) is a fully-worked teaching unit ready to anchor the in-app treatment of Galatians, Romans, and the Judaizer through-line.

**The Mission Statement.** The funding model, the partner ask, the 25/75 split, the math of many hands. This is the page the app's "Tithes, Alms & Offerings" tile links to.

**The Cepher Names and Places Key** (`~/Desktop/Cepher-Names-and-Places-Key-03.22.pdf`). A reference for sacred name spellings and place-name restorations — useful as a cross-check during the restoration pipeline build, not as a source of authority (we do not match the Cepher's choice to omit parentheticals).

**The full live website, mirrored locally — LANDED 2026-05-10.** The entire `remnantofpromise.org` site lives at `~/Desktop/Website/static-site/`. Every page on the live site has its source HTML there: `index.html` (homepage with the four-costumes doorway), `about.html` (What We Teach), `statement-of-faith.html` (the doorway index of all 23 sections), all 23 deep-dive pages at `sof/01-...html` through `sof/23-...html` (with §XI at `sof/11-the-judaizer-error-across-the-ages.html` carrying the apologetic structure for the Christians-as-modern-Judaizers teaching — what most preachers teach, where the inherited reading falls short, how the framework reads it faithfully), all blog posts at `blog/01-...html` through `blog/21-...html` (including `blog/11-the-judaizer-error-has-a-lineage.html` as the paired blog deep-dive), `mission.html`, `partner.html`, `give.html`, `books.html`, `faq.html`, `teaching.html`, `roadmap.html`, `dont-muzzle-the-ox.html`, `contact.html`. Plus markdown source for the Layer 3 expansions at `~/Desktop/Website/sof-layer-3-expansions/` and the WordPress deploy bundle at `~/Desktop/Website/wordpress-deploy/` (which becomes load-bearing in Phase 4 when the SSO architecture is wired). **Session-by-session implication: read the website locally first; do not web-fetch from `remnantofpromise.org`.** Re-fetching from the live site is reserved for confirming a recent edit Yoshi has shipped to production that hasn't yet been pulled back to the local mirror. The Judaizer teaching scope for Section IX Q4 (§XI + the homepage four-costumes doorway + the existing voice-skill major section) is fully in hand from this local mirror.

**The full YouTube teaching corpus, transcript-mirrored locally.** All 13 of Yoshi's catalogued teaching videos have stripped transcripts at `~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/youtube-transcripts/` (and a cleaned set at `~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/youtube-transcripts-cleaned/` with diff trail). The Teaching Corpus's per-concept `from-videos.md` files cite these directly. **Session-by-session implication: do not web-fetch from `youtube.com`.**

**The voice skill.** Twelve Red Lines, the 12-point editorial checklist, the sacred names convention, the Vellum-style heading hygiene, the Judaizer through-line, the cross-and-curse-system framework, the Romans 11 reading, the *son of man → son of Adam* rule. Every line of canon, every line of commentary, every UI string, and every pricing-page word ships through this skill before launch.

**The legal framework.** Remnant of Promise is a 501(c)(3) with bylaws, banking resolution, benevolence policy, and pastoral compensation policy already drafted (`~/Desktop/Remnant of Promise (official documents)/`). Donations through the app route through the existing nonprofit. We do not have to invent the legal home.

We are not starting from zero. We are wiring assets that are already built.

## III. Locked Decisions

Settled with Yoshi. These do not get re-litigated unless Yoshi reopens them.

**Canon scope.** The Protestant 66 (Genesis through Revelation) is the canon and the free tier. Everything beyond — the Apocrypha, Enoch, Jubilees, Jasher, Maccabees, 4 Ezra, 2 Baruch, the Books of Adam and Eve, the Testament of the Twelve Patriarchs, and the rest of the Cepher catalog plus the broader pseudepigrapha we choose to include — sits behind the $4.99 extras tier.

**Platform.** Web-first Progressive Web App. The PWA installs to home screen on iPhone and Android, works offline once installed, supports push notifications, and feels native to a reader who does not dig into the difference. Donations and subscriptions route through Stripe on the web — donations to the 501(c)(3) take 0% platform fee, subscriptions take ~2.9% + 30¢ (versus 15-30% inside Apple's or Google's stores). Native wrappers via Capacitor for the App Store and Play Store come in Phase 2, and they exist for cold discovery only — using Apple's reader-app allowance, the in-store version directs subscribers back to the web for the actual purchase. Desktop comes only if power-study users ask for it.

**Canon-text build (technical).** Zero AI in the canon text itself. Translation, sacred-name restoration, parenthetical insertion, *son of man → son of Adam* substitution, and verse boundaries are all done by a deterministic Python pipeline that codifies the voice skill's rules. Output is byte-stable, reproducible, and verifiable against the existing published Restored Names editions for the books that overlap.

*(Note — 2026-05-14, Session 44: the former "AI policy" clause's study-notes/commentary portion was removed at Yoshi's instruction. How Yoshi uses AI in his own writing and study work — drafting from an outline, editing his notes, working together — is not a project rule, is not a governing policy, and does not belong in this file or on any public surface. It is not anyone's business and is not recorded here. Only the technical, undisputed canon-text build description above is retained.)*

**Funding routing.** The 501(c)(3) is the legal home. Donations route through the assembly's existing nonprofit standing and are tax-deductible. Subscription and tier purchases also route through the nonprofit — the Bylaws and Benevolence Policy already in place cover the appropriations. **Critically: the app does NOT publish auditability claims anywhere user-facing.** Churches under 501(c)(3) are exempt from Form 990 disclosure for sound reasons; voluntary auditability claims create attack surface critics weaponize. The line is between *kingdom-pattern statements* (allowed and welcome) and *auditability claims* (out): a sentence that says *we operate on the older church model — twenty-five cents to the assembly, seventy-five cents back out to the destitute, the widow, the broken roof* is a value statement of how the assembly is structured, not a public-disclosure commitment, and it stays. A sentence that says *the books are open, the accounting is transparent, plainly tracked, every dollar accounted for, come audit us* is the auditability claim that critics can demand receipts on, and that has to leave any user-facing surface — the app, the website, the back-cover copy, the App Store listing, all of it. The Bylaws and Benevolence Policy govern how funds are used internally and are board-overseen; they are not externally published. Refined and clarified 2026-05-10 after Claude's prior overreach (treating the 25/75 ratio statement itself as the problem, when only the books-are-open / plainly-tracked auditability language is). The 25/75 split as a kingdom pattern is in. The auditability frame on top of it is out.

**Pricing — pricing model LOCKED 2026-05-09; tier structure restructured and the ship tiers LOCKED 2026-05-14 (Session 47, see Section IX Q40).** Subscription model only, no one-time purchases. Recurring revenue compounds where one-time purchases cap out, and recurring funds the church's ongoing benevolence work in a way one-time payments cannot.

**The beta ship set is three active tiers — Free / $1.99 / $4.99.** $9.99 and $14.99 are named future tiers, not sold at beta. (This block replaces the original 2026-05-09 five-tier model — Free / $1.99 Study Notes / $4.99 Extras / $9.99 Complete Study / $14.99 Everything — which predated the Q21–Q39 features and the beta-defer-commentary plan. The restructure is recorded in full in the Q40 Session 46 + Session 47 working records.)

- **Free ($0/mo).** The restored Protestant 66; the full public-domain reference apparatus — lexicons (Brown-Driver-Briggs, Thayer's, Gesenius), concordances, Treasury of Scripture Knowledge cross-references, classic public-domain Bible dictionaries (Easton's, Smith's, ISBE 1915), maps and atlases, operator-curated per Q28; Strong's integration; cross-references; basic reader; daily reading plan; search across the free canon; offline (all tiers, per Q23); Statement of Faith doorway (sections I–VII); study guides on the kingdom gospel and the remnant; one highlight color; no notes (all note-taking is paid). Free's "leg up" is the restored text plus the full apparatus — the apparatus is precisely what competitors gate behind a Word Study / $9.99 band, so giving it away free is what makes free out-value their paid tiers on differentiation. Nothing essential paywalled per the Free Truth Principle.
- **$1.99/mo — Notes.** Everything free, plus the Apocrypha (14 books), basic notes, memorization, and export (export = the partner pulling their own notes and highlighted verses out as a document). Free is where you read and study with the full apparatus; $1.99 is where you start working the text yourself. It is the on-ramp — a real first step, deliberately incomplete so the partner feels the next rung. (Named "Notes" per Q21 — describe the contents, no gimmick; the slogan name "make it yours" was tried and rejected by Yoshi, Session 47.)
- **$4.99/mo — Library.** Everything in $1.99 (Apocrypha, basic notes, memorization, export — cumulative), plus the full extra-canonical library (~50 books beyond the canon — Yoshi's four already-published Restored Names editions plus the Shamayim and Ha'avoth collections plus the Books of Adam and Eve plus additional pseudepigrapha, per the extras manifest in Section IV); multiple highlight colors; bookmark organize/label; the smart-notes system; all already-existing extra-canonical framework commentary (after the Q39 voice re-check gate); and the dismantling units (excerpt-only, public-domain targets — per Q40). Build line for the ship date: the ~50 books pipe deterministically and the tooling is code; the dismantling units are authored content and ship as the set written and voice-gated by release, with the remainder rolling in to existing partners free under the permanent price-lock.
- **$9.99 and $14.99 — named future tiers, not at beta.** Reserved for the full-canon (Protestant 66) framework commentary when it is built (Yoshi's open call: it lands at $9.99 *or* as a $4.99 v2), plus live sermons, the courses platform, full Teaching Corpus access, early access to new books, and partner-only bonus content as those surfaces come online. These are not sold at beta and their exact contents are set when they go live.

Four layered moves on top of the tier structure:

- **Annual prepay discount.** Pay annually, get two months free (a ~17% effective discount). Standard sub-industry practice; reduces churn; locks in committed partners for a year. Applies to the $1.99 and $4.99 ship tiers and to the future tiers when they launch.
- **Founder pricing — NEEDS REVISIT (flagged 2026-05-14, Session 47).** The original founder offer locked the first 1,000 Everything-tier partners at $9.99/mo for life. Everything is not a beta tier, so the founder offer must be re-pointed at a tier that actually ships ($4.99, or a founder rate on it) before launch. Open follow-up under Q40.
- **Seven-day free trial on any paid tier.** Standard friction-removal at the decision point. The text and commentary sell themselves once readers try them.
- **Permanent price-lock for every subscriber, on every tier — LOCKED 2026-05-10.** Once a partner is on a tier, that's their tier price for life. No surprise increases, no "we made the tier better so it costs more now" upcharges. The price they signed up at is the price they keep, forever. Doable for the assembly because we optimize for partners served, not revenue per user; marginal cost of adding the millionth partner to a digital app is essentially zero. It's a commitment Logos, Olive Tree, and Accordance structurally cannot match, because their cost basis grows with their org and ours does not. It's the cost-structure asymmetry made explicit at the partner level — and it's the second of four strategic implications under the cost-structure asymmetry principle in `~/Desktop/Roadmap/Growth_Strategy.md`. Pricing-page copy will say it plainly: *"The price you pay today is the price you pay forever. We will never raise it on you."* It also means the $4.99 dismantling-units roll-out and the future-tier content reach existing partners without an upcharge.

The competitive frame: Logos has no tier between Free and $9.99/mo, and runs $9.99 / $14.99 / $19.99 above that. Olive Tree premium is $9.99–$19.99/mo. Accordance starts at $8/mo. None of them have sacred-name restoration, none have Yoshi's framework commentary, none have the Statement of Faith going twenty-three sections deep. RoP's $1.99 and $4.99 bands are uncontested — no competitor sells there at all — and RoP free wins on differentiation (the restored text plus the framework) rather than on volume. The head-to-head, when the future tiers launch, is RoP $9.99 vs. Logos Premium $9.99.

**Voice gate.** Every line of canon text, every commentary entry, every UI string, every pricing-page word, every onboarding step, every push notification ships through the yoshi-voice skill before launch. The 12-point editorial checklist and the 12 Red Lines are the standard. No exceptions for "it's just UI copy" — the app is a teaching surface, every word counts.

**Architecture — subdomain on the existing site, single sign-on from day one.** The Bible app lives at `bible.remnantofpromise.org`. WordPress keeps `remnantofpromise.org` itself (homepage, Statement of Faith, blog, books page, Mission Statement, Tithes-Alms-and-Offerings flow, the assembly's social platform). The two are technically independent services that share a domain, a brand, and a logo — neither one fights the other for the runtime it needs. Single sign-on is wired from day one: WordPress is the identity provider, the Bible app consumes its JWT — one login that works on both sides. Donations route through the existing `/give` flow on the WordPress side (one channel for the bookkeeping); subscriptions and extras purchases route through Stripe on the PWA side. App Store and Play Store wrappers in Phase 2 point at the same `bible.remnantofpromise.org` URL. One brand, one home, one church, one funding pipeline.

*Note 2026-05-10 session 9.* A mid-session conversation explored pulling the social platform out of WordPress onto its own subdomain (`community.remnantofpromise.org`) with native app-store wrappers, framework-anchored governance, Kickstarter funding for the build, and a tithe-path pricing model where $100/month tithers receive every paid surface free. Yoshi's call: not now. Bible app must launch first and start funding the assembly before the social platform's build is taken on. The full architectural exploration, the governance principles, the Kickstarter framing, the tithe-path math, and the cost picture are parked at `~/Desktop/future social media enterprise/IDEAS_OUTLINE.md` — not lost, just shelved until the Bible app is generating revenue and a Kickstarter campaign is timed to launch. Social presence stays on WordPress in the meantime.

## IV. Open Questions for Yoshi

These need a Yoshi answer before the relevant phase can land. Not blocking the start of work, but blocking the close of the relevant phase.

**Naming — LOCKED 2026-05-09.** *The Remnant of Promise Official Study Bible.* Yoshi's reasoning: since discovery flows through remnantofpromise.org and the existing audience (not through cold App Store keyword search), the title doesn't need to carry SEO weight on the *restored names* phrase — the app description and the website do that. Cleanest name on the spine, on the home tile, in the App Store listing. The "restored sacred names with parentheticals" feature lives in the description and the onboarding flow.

**Pricing model — model LOCKED 2026-05-09; tiers restructured and ship tiers LOCKED 2026-05-14 (Session 47).** Subscription-only, monthly. The beta ship set is three active tiers — Free / $1.99 / $4.99 — with $9.99 and $14.99 as named future tiers not sold at beta. Annual prepay discount and seven-day free trial apply; founder pricing needs a revisit (it was tied to the Everything tier, which is not a beta tier). Full structure detailed in the rewritten Section III pricing block above and in the Q40 Session 46 + 47 working records.

**Extras manifest — LOCKED 2026-05-09 (working list).** The extras tier definitive list: Yoshi's four already-published Restored Names editions (Apocrypha — 14 books, Jasher, Enoch, Jubilees), plus the Shamayim collection from cepher.net (Brit HaTorah, Apocalypse of Peter, Vision of Paul, 2 Enoch, Apocalypse of Moses, Apocalypse of Abraham, Alphabet of David, Alphabet of Ben Sirach, Testaments of the Twelve Patriarchs, 2 Baruch, 3 Baruch, Ascension of Isaiah — about 14 books beyond canon overlap), plus the Ha'avoth collection from cepher.net (Didache, Gospel of Mary, Gospel of James / Protoevangelium, Gospel of Nicodemus / Acts of Pilate, Gospel of Peter, Epistle of Barnabas, Shepherd of Hermas in three parts, 1 Clement, 2 Clement, Martyrdom of Polycarp, seven letters of Ignatius — about 20 books), plus Books of Adam and Eve I & II (Malan 1882). Approximately 50 books total beyond the canon. All public-domain English bases — predominantly Charles' 1913 *Apocrypha and Pseudepigrapha* and Lightfoot's 1885 *Apostolic Fathers* and Schaff's *Ante-Nicene Fathers*. Detailed source mapping in `source-texts/SOURCE_TEXT_INVENTORY.md`.

**Christians-as-Judaizers — elevate or leave.** This insight is already in the voice skill (a major section), on the homepage in plain words, and at depth in §XI of the Statement of Faith. Three places. Yoshi asked me to "save it to the voice skill" — it is saved. Question: does Yoshi want this elevated into its own foregrounded teaching unit inside the app's commentary surface (a featured tile, a default first-read after the doorway), or is its current placement enough? Default action if Yoshi does not specify: feature it as one of the three "first reads" on the app's home tile after onboarding.

## V. The Phase Plan

Phases map to the TaskList for this project. Each phase has a definition of done; a phase does not close until the deliverable is in known-good state and Yoshi has signed off where indicated.

### Phase 0 — Foundation & decisions
Done when: the App folder exists with this roadmap, the existing assets are audited, the Statement of Faith is reviewed, the three lock-down decisions are settled with Yoshi (canon scope, platform, AI policy). **Status: in progress, closing this session.**

### Phase 1 — This roadmap, plus naming and pricing locks
Done when: this roadmap is written, the naming question is settled, the pricing question is settled (tiered first / subscription as Phase-2 upsell, or Yoshi's override), and the extras manifest is definitive.

### Phase 2 — Source text inventory and ingestion
Done when: every public-domain source we need is in `source-texts/` with a clear provenance record. KJV verse text (the base for the canon, public domain), Strong's Hebrew dictionary, Strong's Greek dictionary, Strong's tagging on the KJV (Tregelles / Online Bible / Sword Project public-domain tagging exists), the existing Restored Names editions imported as the validation target for the pipeline, and the extras manifest's source texts pulled from public-domain sources (most of the extras have public-domain English translations from the late 1800s — Charles for Enoch, the Apocryphal Books of the New Testament editions for the Adam and Eve / Twelve Patriarchs material, etc.).

### Phase 3 — Restoration pipeline (sacred names + parentheticals + son of Adam)
Done when: a deterministic Python script takes any input English text and produces the restored output, applying every rule in the yoshi-voice skill — Yahuah (God) on every mention, Yahusha (Jesus) on every mention, Yashar'el (Israel), Yahudah (Judah), Yahudim (Jews), Yahudi (Jew/Jewish), Elohim (God), Messiah (Christ), Ruach HaKodesh (Holy Spirit), Yah without parenthetical, *Son of Adam* every time *son of man* appears, capitalized as the original capitalized. The pipeline runs on the KJV Apocrypha and produces output byte-equivalent (or with documented intentional variance) to the published *Apocrypha — Restored Names Edition*. Same validation against Jasher, Enoch, Jubilees as those were already restored. Once the pipeline matches Yoshi's already-published work on the books we have ground truth for, it gets run on the rest of the canon and extras and the output goes into Phase 4.

### Phase 4 — Data schema, app shell, subdomain, single sign-on
Done when: the data model is laid down (books, chapters, verses, Strong's tags, cross-references, commentary entries, study notes, user accounts, subscription tiers, purchase records — donations are NOT in this schema, they route to WordPress) and the web PWA shell is standing at `bible.remnantofpromise.org` — a basic reader for one book of the canon, served from the schema, installable to home screen, working offline. React + Tailwind on Vite for the front, Postgres for the data, FastAPI Python backend (matches the restoration pipeline's runtime). DNS A-record / CNAME on `bible.remnantofpromise.org` pointed at the PWA host (Vercel, Fly, Render, or DigitalOcean App Platform — final hosting choice in this phase). SSL via Let's Encrypt or the host's automatic provisioning. **Single sign-on wired in this phase, not deferred.** WordPress is the identity provider; we install a JWT plugin on the WordPress side (JWT Authentication for WP REST API, or similar mature option), the PWA's auth flow redirects to WordPress to log in, WordPress issues a JWT, the PWA stores it and uses it for API calls. One login, two surfaces, partner sees no seam. Stripe wired for a single test purchase tier so we know the payment path works end-to-end (full pricing model lands in Phase 7).

### Phase 5 — Strong's lookup integration
Done when: tap-on-word in the reader opens the Strong's entry for that word's underlying Hebrew or Greek lemma, with the lexicon definition, the lemma's other occurrences in the canon, and a one-tap "show me all the verses where this same lemma appears" cross-reference. Public-domain Strong's data is already indexed; this phase wires the UI.

**Flagship feature, earmarked session 17 (2026-05-11): the nikkudot-strip + grouped-column view.** A toggle in the Strong's surface that takes out all the Masoretic vowel-points and shows every word with the same consonantal form in a column, with definitions beside them — so the reader can see the truth of the words and the Masoretic error at a glance. The Masoretic pointing tradition disambiguated identical consonantal roots into distinct lexical entries by editorial choice; the framework reading is that a portion of those choices obscured connections the unpointed text preserved. The grouped-column view, with nikkudot off, makes the connection visible without requiring the reader to read Hebrew. Yoshi: *"a must have feature."* Wireframe + data-model design folded into Phase 5; ships at Phase 5 close or as a Phase-5 follow-on if the basic tap-on-word lookup ships first.

### Phase 6 — Commentary surface from the Teaching Corpus
Done when: the verse → concept index is built (which concepts touch which verses, drawn from the `verses.md` files inside each Teaching Corpus concept folder) and the reader surfaces "Yoshi's framework reading on this passage" inline. The commentary text comes from the concept summaries Yoshi has approved; the AI layer (semantic search, cross-references, Q&A) is grounded in the same corpus. Nothing is generated. The Statement of Faith sections are surfaced as the deeper-dive layer behind any commentary entry that touches their themes.

### Phase 7 — Subscription tiers, accounts, payments
Done when: the locked subscription model is wired per the rewritten Section III pricing block — the three active ship tiers (Free / $1.99 / $4.99 monthly), with $9.99 and $14.99 carried as named future tiers — Stripe Subscriptions handles the recurring billing on the web side, donations route to the 501(c)(3) via the existing remnantofpromise.org/give flow on the WordPress side (NOT in this app — that path takes 0% platform fee and lives in WordPress), and the in-app *Tithes, Alms & Offerings* tile mirrors the website's tone and deep-links into the WordPress giving flow with SSO so the partner doesn't sign in twice. The annual prepay discount (2 months free) is implemented as an alternate Stripe price ID per paid tier. The founder-pricing path is implemented as a separate Stripe price ID locked in for life via the customer's subscription metadata — and per the Session 47 flag, the founder offer must be re-pointed at a tier that ships at beta (it was originally tied to the Everything tier, which is not a beta tier). The seven-day free trial is wired on every paid tier via Stripe's trial_period_days. Tier-gated content access is enforced server-side via JWT claims on every API call (the SSO from Phase 4 carries the entitlement set in the JWT body). The App Store / Play Store wrappers (Phase 2 deliverable from launch) include the Apple "external link account entitlement" and Google "User Choice Billing" buttons that route the subscriber to the web for the actual purchase, keeping ~97% of subscription revenue with the church instead of 70-85%.

### Phase 8 — Voice-skill verification (ongoing discipline, not pre-launch hard gate)
**Reframed per Yoshi's correction 2026-05-09.** Not a perfectionist gate before launch. An ongoing verification discipline that runs in parallel with shipping. Version 1 ships when the foundation is right (canon restored, sacred names enforced, Yoshi-rendered passages applied, basic UI works, payment plumbing functional). Errors found post-launch get fixed in version 1.1, 1.2, etc. Partners' trust is anchored on the foundation, not on zero-defects. The verification discipline runs continuously: every text change passes the 12-point editorial checklist and the 12 Red Lines; every restored verse is checked against the voice skill (not against Cepher or any external Restored Names edition — the voice skill is the standard, externals are data); a subagent runs periodic independent audit passes; reader-reported errors are triaged, fixed, and the affected version released within a defined SLA (target: high-severity within 7 days, low-severity within 30 days). The publish-then-edit model is what this Phase encodes.

*Note 2026-05-10 session 9.* A future Phase 9 — the community platform at `community.remnantofpromise.org` with framework-anchored governance and Kickstarter funding — was explored mid-session and parked at `~/Desktop/future social media enterprise/IDEAS_OUTLINE.md`. Activated only after the Bible app is generating revenue and a Kickstarter is timed to fund the build.

## VI. Standing Rules

Apply throughout the build. Violations get caught at Phase 8 if not earlier.

**The voice gate is non-negotiable.** Every line of text the app shows the reader passes the voice-skill standard. The 12 Red Lines and the 12-point editorial checklist are the test. The 1234 of Truth is the master filter. If a sentence pits faith against Torah, the sentence is wrong. If a passage uses the Jew-and-Gentile binary as the framework, the passage is wrong. If a "come home" section prescribes practice, the section is wrong. Every place the canon or the commentary touches Galatians, Romans, or the Judaizer question, the through-line traces Pharisees → Galatian agitators → modern Christianity → modern Hebrew Roots false inclusion → Islam — same flesh-credential gospel, four costumes, one antichrist.

**The voice skill is the standard — external Restored Names editions are DATA, not authority.** Cepher, ISR's Scriptures, Halleluyah Scriptures, Pidgeon's work, and the Greek and Hebrew lexicons themselves are sources we read; the voice skill is the lens we read them through. Verification flow: *check this against the voice skill, not check this against Cepher.* When an external source agrees with the voice skill, that is confirmation. When an external source disagrees, the voice skill wins — and we should be FINDING the external source's errors using the framework, not absorbing them. This applies especially to passages where the standard religious systems (Catholic, Protestant, Hebrew Roots, Messianic) and the standard Restored Names publishers have all inherited the same error from a common source. The voice skill stands above every external source, every translation, every lexicon. The framework is the gold standard.

**Publish-then-edit, not perfect-before-launch.** Yoshi's published-book track record (seventeen books in three months) demonstrates the model: ship work that has the foundation right, find errors in subsequent passes, fix and republish. The work that gets published gathers partners; the work that waits for perfect-before-launch never gets there. With the voice skill as the foundation, even significant errors read to the partner as minor contradictions or "maybe I misunderstood" moments — not as red flags about the work's integrity. Phase 8 (voice-skill verification) is therefore an ongoing discipline rather than a perfectionist hard-gate before launch. Ship version 1 when the foundation is right and the basics work. Find errors. Fix. Ship version 1.1. Trust the foundation to carry the partner through the editing iterations.

**Apply judgment, land the work, Yoshi reviews at project completion — do not gate on inline approvals.** Locked 2026-05-10 session 4 after Yoshi caught the bottleneck: *"i approve things at completion of project so ask questions here and apply them, i dont have time to go read over everything you write the moment you write it, that takes away from efficiency."* The drafting-then-await-approval pattern (DRAFT files in `_scratch/` queued for Yoshi's read-and-bless before landing) was forcing Yoshi to read his own thinking twice — once when he wrote the original instruction, again when Claude expanded it — and was the wrong architecture. The correct pattern: Claude applies the standing voice gate plus the path-decision authority granted in Section IX, lands the work in place, logs what landed and why in Section X, and Yoshi reviews at session close or project completion. Open questions still get logged in Section IX and surface in the next-session handoff prompt; they don't block work that the locked path-authority covers. The supercomputer is supposed to be doing 100x the work, not asking the user to re-read what he just wrote.

**The voice skill is a non-contradiction filter, not a content roadmap.** Locked 2026-05-10 session 4 after Yoshi caught Claude treating the skill as a template to weave into every output: *"voice skill is not a roadmap, its to make sure nothing contradicts it, its not insert this and every aspect of it into every single thing you write."* The skill is the lens. A piece of writing passes the gate when nothing in it contradicts the framework — not when every framework piece is present. Mission Statement language, app commentary, UI strings, study-note prose, and any other deliverable should be evaluated for *whether the writing contradicts anything in the skill*, not for *whether the writing exhibits everything in the skill*. The Red Lines and the 12-point editorial checklist are pass/fail standards on contradiction, not coverage requirements. When drafting, write the cleanest piece that says what the piece needs to say; thread framework references only where the piece's argument requires them. Threading the entire framework into every paragraph produces dense, exhausting copy and misses the point of having a focused deliverable.

**The roadmap is sequential, not a fork.** Locked 2026-05-10 session 5 after Yoshi caught Claude opening session 5 with *"which would you like me to advance — Phase 3 iteration, Phase 4 opening, or off-roadmap?"* — and again, fairly, said *"how is it that a roadmap leaves you wondering wich direction to turn? i thought you built a roadmap that takes you from point a to z with all the inbetween lined out."* He's right. The roadmap is the path from A to Z. Phases close in order. When the current phase is open, the work to do is the next iteration of the current phase — not a fork between this phase and the next. Path-decision authority being locked to Claude (Section IX) means Claude *follows the path the roadmap already lays down,* not that Claude asks the user to choose between phases the roadmap has already ordered. The session-4 handoff prompt itself wrote the question as a fork — that was the same mistake one session removed and is now corrected: handoff prompts name *the next wheel,* not *a menu of wheels.*

**One wheel, not the whole truck.** Locked 2026-05-10 session 5 from Yoshi's truck-washing story, told plainly: *"i washed all over that truck for an hour and it still looked like an old junky truck… he told me to wash a wheel, he said if that wheel takes two hours, when someone checks, they can say i cleaned that wheel, its what i did."* The discipline: when a phase has multiple parallel pieces (e.g., Phase 3 has four book diffs and YOSHI_RENDERED_PASSAGES integration and rule extensions), do not spread effort thin across all of them in one session. Pick **one bounded piece** — the wheel — and finish it end-to-end so the user can look at it and say *that piece is clean.* When the wheel is done, the next wheel is the next session's work. Session 5 demonstrated the pattern: one wheel was the **Apocrypha validation diff**, scrubbed end-to-end (harness built, run, categorized, rule fixes landed, self-tests expanded, idempotency confirmed, intentional variances documented, published-edition gaps logged) — Jasher / Jubilees / Enoch wheels deferred to subsequent sessions on purpose. Hosing the whole truck for an hour produces *thin progress everywhere and clean nothing.* The wheel produces a piece the user can verify is actually finished.

**Session-length hard stop discipline.** Long sessions accumulate errors as context fills and attention degrades. The discipline: at roughly 25-30 substantive turns or when context budget passes ~70% capacity, Claude wraps the session cleanly with a checkpoint summary and a batch of questions for the next session, rather than pushing further. Yoshi noticed this principle before Claude did in the May 9 session — the books-are-open language slipped through despite being a clear voice-skill violation, traceable directly to session fatigue. The hard-stop is now policy. Whichever side notices first calls the wrap.

*Wrap-signal sharpening, refined 2026-05-10.* The wrap signals are specific and narrow: (a) substantive-turn count at or beyond the 25-30 range, (b) context budget at or beyond ~70%, or (c) observable error-rate threshold — multiple corrections in close succession on similar work, indicating attention degradation. **A *feeling* that the session has gone long is not a wrap signal.** Catching and correcting a single overreach is the system working as designed, not a fatigue failure — and not a wrap trigger by itself. Conservative early wraps are the failure mode this rule is now also guarding against, because they are the mechanism by which work that should land *this* session gets pushed to the next session and potentially lost in the verbal-to-file transcription gap. Honor what the rule says, not what reflex says. If a wrap is being called and none of the three triggers above is actually present, name it as *I'm choosing to wrap early because <reason>* and let Yoshi decide whether to override.

**Local files first — do not web-fetch what's already on disk.** Yoshi's content surfaces are mirrored locally before they're touched. The full live website is at `~/Desktop/Website/static-site/`. The full YouTube teaching corpus has stripped transcripts at `~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/youtube-transcripts/`. The published Restored Names editions are at `~/Desktop/App/source-texts/existing-restored-editions/` and at the original book-project folders on the Desktop. Public-domain source texts for the extras manifest live (where already pulled) at `~/Desktop/App/source-texts/`. Before any `mcp__workspace__web_fetch` or `WebSearch` call against a domain we own, Claude checks the local mirrors first — and reaches for the web only when (a) the local mirror is missing or (b) Yoshi has explicitly published a recent change to the live site that hasn't yet been pulled back. This rule was caught and locked 2026-05-10 after Claude went to web-fetch §XI before checking the Desktop, hit an egress allowlist wall, and listed permission options instead of looking locally. Yoshi caught it. The rule is now policy.

**When a permission wall blocks the work, ask directly for the permission needed — do not stop and report.** Pair rule with *Local files first.* When the local-files-first check confirms the content actually does need to come from a network resource and the egress allowlist blocks the fetch, Claude asks Yoshi directly to add the specific domain to the allowlist (Settings → Capabilities) — naming the domain, naming the work it unblocks, naming whether it's a one-time need or recurring. Same pattern with the directory-mount permission: ask for the broadest reasonable scope that clears the work (Yoshi's instruction 2026-05-10: *"if i give you a broad enough permission you can work freely"*). Reporting the wall and listing options for Yoshi to choose between is not the right move; asking for the permission and trusting Yoshi to grant or refuse is.

**Verbal answers transcribe to source files at session close — non-negotiable.** When Yoshi answers an open question or settles a decision in conversation rather than by editing a file, Claude must write that answer back into the source location where the question lives — Section IX of this roadmap, the YOSHI_RENDERED_PASSAGES registry, the source-text inventory, the voice skill update queue, the Growth Strategy doc, whichever applies — before the session is presented as closed. The session log entry in Section X must reference where each verbal answer landed in writing. The rule exists because the next session opens by reading the roadmap, not by reading the prior session transcript. Answers that live only in conversation are answers that get lost. The session-length hard stop and this rule travel as a pair: the wrap-up checkpoint is not just "summarize and ask the next batch"; it is "transcribe everything that was settled into the file that holds it, then summarize and ask the next batch." If the transcription step is skipped, the hard stop has not actually been honored — the session has just been ended early. Caught and locked 2026-05-10 after session 2 opened against an apparently-empty Section IX that had in fact been answered across session 1's conversation.

**Sacred name parentheticals on every mention, every time, in body text.** The convention from the voice skill applies: Yahuah (God), Yahusha (Jesus), Elohim (God), Messiah (Christ), Ruach HaKodesh (Holy Spirit), Yashar'el (Israel), Yahudah (Judah), Yahudim (Jews), Yahudi (Jew), Yahudi (Jewish), **Melek Tsadiq (Melchizedek)** [LANDED 2026-05-09 — replaces all standard *Melchizedek* renderings; two Hebrew words preserved with parenthetical English on every mention; pipeline-enforced]. Yah needs no parenthetical. Other biblical human and city names: either form acceptable, translator's call, stay consistent within a passage. Back matter (legal, About, contact, terms of service) uses conventional titles to meet the reader where they are. The one exception — About the Author — uses restored names with parentheticals because the testimony carries the name's weight.

**Son of Adam, everywhere.** Wherever the source text says *son of man,* the restored output says *Son of Adam* (Messianic title) or *son of Adam* (prophetic address or poetic generic). No misses. The restoration pipeline catches this; the voice gate verifies it.

**No prescription.** The app is a Bible study tool, not a how-to manual. Commentary names what the text says, dismantles the inherited reading, points the reader to the Word and the Father, and stops there. We do not say *light a candle Friday at sundown.* We say *the seventh day was hallowed in the beginning, the horn moved it, the prophets say all flesh will gather on it.* The reader walks from there with the Father and the Word.

**Victims, not enemies.** Any time the commentary touches the Catholic, Protestant, Hebrew Roots, Messianic, or Islamic reader, the posture is patience, not condescension. The grandmother who hung the cross loved the Father; the system that gave her the cross is what we dismantle. The systems are the enemy. The people are the lost sheep.

**Two-file rule for any text deliverables Yoshi will read.** When this project produces a document Yoshi reads — a study notes draft, a teaching unit, a commentary write-up — the document follows the voice skill's two-file convention: one `<Title>.docx` for the content, one `<Title>_NOTES.md` for the standing rules and session log. We do not stack `_v2`, `_v3`, dated duplicates. Save over original. Code and source-text folders are not subject to this rule (those are infrastructure).

**Vellum-clean if any printed companion volumes are produced.** If the app spawns a printed study Bible volume or a printed commentary collection, it must follow the Vellum heading hygiene from the voice skill — `Heading1` for chapters, `Heading2` for subsections, `w:pPr` element ordering preserved, no markdown asterisks in the .docx text content. The `fix_vellum.py` pattern is the reference.

**Free Truth Principle holds inside the app.** From the Statement of Faith: nothing essential is paywalled. The doorway gospel is free. The canon is free. Yoshi's commentary on the canon — the framework reading at the verse level — is paid. The reader who downloads the free version gets the gospel as a confession, the Bible to read, the Statement of Faith as the doorway, and the doorway version of the commentary that names what is happening without going to depth. Going to depth is what the paid tier funds.

**Honest naming — Restored Names Edition, not "translation."** This app's text is a *Restored Names Edition* and *revision* of public-domain English bases (KJV for the canon, Charles 1912 for Enoch and Jubilees, Noah 1840 for Jasher, KJV 1611 for the Apocrypha set, Charles vol. translations for the rest of the extras). It is NOT a translation in the sense of fresh rendering from Hebrew or Greek source manuscripts — and we will not call it one. The work is enormously valuable as a Restored Names Edition, but the word *translation* has a specific meaning, and the Hebrew Roots audience is sophisticated enough to call out misuse. Honest framing protects credibility and matches what Yoshi's published Restored Names Editions already use. The one exception: any specific passage where Yoshi has personally done direct work from Hebrew or Greek source manuscripts may be marked as *Yoshi's rendering from the Hebrew (or Greek)* on that passage, with the surrounding text remaining Restored Names KJV. A future fresh translation from source manuscripts — Yoshi's framework reading combined with the lexical and grammatical apparatus the academy has built — is a separate project on the other side of this app, and a worthy one. This app is not that project. Marketing copy, About-This-Edition pages, App Store and Play Store descriptions, and back-cover copy on any printed companion volumes all use *Restored Names Edition* and *our edition* / *our text* / *our work* — never *our translation.*

**Yoshi-rendered passages — the targeted-correction registry.** A specific category of passages where the KJV is demonstrably wrong (collapses two distinct Greek or Hebrew words into one English word, mis-renders a key technical term, or otherwise loses the meaning of the source). These passages do NOT pass through the standard modernize-and-restore pipeline; they get Yoshi-direct-from-source renderings that carry a footnote explaining the Greek or Hebrew and why the standard English misleads. The footnote serves as the apologetic — any reader questioning *why is your text different from the KJV here?* gets the answer on the same screen. The registry is maintained at `restoration-pipeline/YOSHI_RENDERED_PASSAGES.md`. As of session 1, the seed entries are:

- **1 Corinthians 11:2-16 (head coverings)** — KJV uses one English word *covering* for two distinct Greek words: *katakalúptō* (vv 4-13) is the verb meaning to put a covering ON TOP of something, while *peribólaion* (v 15) is a noun meaning a wrap or natural endowment. Verse 15 saying a woman's hair is given to her *as a peribólaion* doesn't mean she doesn't need *katakalúptō* — it means her hair is what the additional covering goes on top of. The KJV's flattening to a single English word destroys Paul's argument and produces the inherited reading "her hair is her covering so she doesn't need one" — which is logically incoherent inside the same passage. Yoshi-rendered to preserve the two Greek words distinctly in English with a footnote.

- **Psalm 81:3 (the dark / concealed moon)** — Hebrew *keseh* shares its root with *kasah* meaning *to cover, to conceal*. The literal force is *the concealed moon* — the dark moon at astronomical conjunction, not the first visible sliver that later rabbinic tradition uses to start the month. Translators flattened it to "new moon" or "full moon" without preserving the concealment. Yoshi-rendered as something like *the moon's concealment* or *the dark moon* with a footnote explaining the Hebrew root and why the choice matters for the feast calendar.

- (More to be added as Yoshi flags them through the project.)

**Critically: we do not copy Cepher's English on these passages.** Cepher correctly identifies many of these problems and renders them in a particular way, but Cepher's English is Cepher's editorial work and copying it is plagiarism. We do the SAME theological move (rendering both Greek words distinctly, restoring the Hebrew concealment-meaning) using OUR OWN English derived from the source-language analysis. Where two Restored Names Editions land on the same correction, that's convergence on what the source actually says — not borrowing.

**Cold-storage backup — a physical disk in a vault, not cloud-only.** Yoshi's requirement (2026-05-14): the whole project must be backed up to physical, offline, cold storage — a disk in a vault — not trusted solely to a cloud or a server. The instinct is sound: a disconnected offline copy is immune to the failure modes cloud and server backups share — account compromise, a provider losing or deleting the account, ransomware reaching connected systems, a billing lapse, a company folding. Refinement so the vault disk is not itself a single point of failure: it is the *last-resort cold copy*, kept *in addition to* the working redundancy (the private GitHub repo, the live Desktop copies), and ideally it is more than one drive — two disks, refreshed on a schedule, stored where practical in two separate locations — because a single unpowered drive can degrade and a single vault can burn or flood. Scope of "everything": the git repo, `source-texts/` (raw + processed + existing-restored-editions), the parsed JSONs, the restoration pipeline, the data schema, the `app/` codebase, the commentary, this roadmap, the website mirror, and the `yoshi-voice.skill` bundle plus its version archive. This is an operational discipline Yoshi performs on his own hardware; the roadmap records the requirement and the recommended shape so it isn't lost. Worth a first cold backup sooner rather than later — 42 sessions of work already exist.

## VII. File Structure

```
~/Desktop/App/
├── BIBLE_APP_ROADMAP.md          ← this file
├── source-texts/                  ← public-domain sources, raw and processed
│   ├── kjv/                       ← KJV verse text, Strong's-tagged
│   ├── strongs-hebrew/            ← public-domain Strong's Hebrew lexicon
│   ├── strongs-greek/             ← public-domain Strong's Greek lexicon
│   ├── extras/                    ← public-domain English of Enoch, Adam & Eve, etc.
│   └── existing-restored-editions/ ← reference copies from Yoshi's KDP work
├── restoration-pipeline/          ← the deterministic Python script
├── data-schema/                   ← table designs, indexing, Strong's tagging
├── app/                           ← the web PWA (React + Tailwind on Vite)
├── commentary/                    ← Yoshi's study notes, organized by book/passage
├── _inbox/                        ← captured material awaiting placement
└── _scratch/                      ← working drafts, temporary files
```

Top-level documents in `~/Desktop/App/` are for Yoshi to read. Folders are infrastructure. The Teaching Corpus at `~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/` is referenced, not duplicated — the in-app commentary projects from there.

---

## VIII. Where This Sits in the Larger Mission

The Mission Statement names ten thousand partners contributing one hundred dollars a month. Twenty-five percent funds the assembly's operations — including this app's development, hosting, and maintenance. Seventy-five percent goes back out to the destitute, the widow, the homeless, the broken roof, the brother whose car broke down, the interest-free loan up to ten thousand dollars on Deuteronomy 23:19 terms. The app is one of the channels that gathers those ten thousand partners. It is not the work; it serves the work.

The reader who downloads the free Bible meets Yoshi's voice in the Statement of Faith doorway, opens the canon with restored names, taps a verse and reads the framework reading, and at some point sees the *Tithes, Alms & Offerings* tile and reads what the assembly does with what comes in. *If the work has fed you, partner with it.* Some readers become partners. The partners fund the next thing — the printed study Bible, the home repair team, the destitute member's loan.

This is the engine. The app feeds the gathering. The gathering feeds the work. The work is the proof.

**11. Apocalyptic compound divine titles in 1 Enoch — render as bare *Yahuah (Lord) of X*, or as a Hebrew compound?** Surfaced 2026-05-10 session 8 during the Enoch wheel. The published *Book of Enoch — Restored Names Edition* uses Charles 1912's English titles *Lord of Spirits* (45 instances in the Parables, chs 37–69), *Lord of the sheep* (33 instances in the Animal Apocalypse, chs 85–90), and *Lord of lords* (9:4) for divine reference. Pipeline currently restores these conservatively — *Lord* → *Yahuah (Lord)* with the English compound tail kept — producing *"Yahuah (Lord) of Spirits,"* *"Yahuah (Lord) of the sheep,"* *"Yahuah (Lord) of lords."* The conservative path was deliberate: *Yahuah Tsabaoth (Lord of Hosts)* is a real canonical Hebrew compound (יהוה צבאות, "Yahuah of armies"), but it is a *different* divine title from 1 Enoch's *Lord of Spirits* — substituting Tsabaoth would silently swap one title for another and lose 1 Enoch's specific apocalyptic identifier. A constructed *Yahuah Ha'Ruchot (Lord of Spirits)* would render the apocalyptic title in Hebrew form, but that's a Yoshi-rendered-passage call (a framework decision about what the Hebrew compound *should* be for an apocalyptic title we only have downstream Greek / Ge'ez witnesses for), not a pipeline rule Claude can land on his own authority. Same architectural question for *Lord of the sheep* (potential Hebrew compound: *Yahuah Ro'eh (Lord Shepherd)* or similar) and for the bare *Lord called me* of 81:3 (currently restores to *Yahuah (Lord)* per the standard rule). Question to Yoshi: keep current conservative pipeline behavior, or elevate one or more of these apocalyptic compound divine titles to Yoshi-rendered Hebrew compound forms in the registry?

→ **Pending.** Yoshi: *"how do you translate lord of the spirit, are you using yahuah tsebaot or whatever?"* Claude explained the conservative pipeline behavior and surfaced the elevation question. Yoshi did not specify a direction this session. Default behavior holds: pipeline produces *Yahuah (Lord) of Spirits* / *Yahuah (Lord) of the sheep* / *Yahuah (Lord) of lords* until Yoshi names an elevation. Question carries forward; the punch-list for the published Enoch .docx revision in `INTENTIONAL_VARIANCES.md` reflects the current pipeline output.

*Note 2026-05-10 session 9.* Mid-session conversation explored the community-platform architecture (own subdomain, framework-anchored governance, Kickstarter funding, tithe-path pricing). Open questions that would have lived here — TOS drafting timeline, partner-elder delegation model, real-time backend stack, founder-pricing pool overlap — are parked at `~/Desktop/future social media enterprise/IDEAS_OUTLINE.md` until the community platform's Phase opens. Not lost; deferred.

**12. `verses` table schema extension — `source_type` and `footnote_id` columns.** Surfaced 2026-05-11 session 15 when the Yoshi-rendering substitution hook landed in `parse_canon.py`. The pipeline now produces `canon.json` with every overridden verse tagged `source_type: yoshi-rendering` and the chapter footnote attached at the chapter level, but the DB schema in `data-schema/schema.sql` carries only `(id, chapter_id, verse_number, text)` on the verses table. `seed.py` writes only the verse text to the DB. The chapter-level commentary survives the trip (it lands in `chapters.chapter_intro`), so the chapter footnote is queryable. But the per-verse `source_type` and the per-verse `footnote_id` (the link back to the chapter footnote for the small UI marker the registry preamble names) do NOT make it to the DB. For the immediate goal of *the rendering is the default text served from the API for 1 Cor 11*, this doesn't matter — the verse text in the DB is the locked Yoshi rendering. For the future UI work of *show a small marker on Yoshi-rendered verses and link to the chapter footnote on tap,* the schema needs `verses.source_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'restored-names-kjv'` and `verses.footnote_id TEXT` columns added, `seed.py`'s `insert_verses` updated to write those fields, and a `--bootstrap` migration (or in-place ALTER for an existing DB). Pick this up in Phase 5/6 when the verse-marker UI design is being built.

→ **Deferred to Phase 5/6.** Default behavior in the meantime: the rendering serves correctly (text is the locked Yoshi text). The marker UI cannot fire from the DB until the schema carries the tag. Verification script `~/Desktop/App/api/verify_yoshi_renderings.py` checks the verse text via substring match, which works without the column. When the schema work is picked up: extend `verses` schema, extend `seed.py`'s `insert_verses` to carry `source_type` and `footnote_id`, regenerate `canon.json` (already carries the fields in the JSON shape), re-seed, and the marker UI has the data it needs.

**13. Strong's surface — the nikkudot-strip + grouped-column view as a Phase-5 flagship feature.** Surfaced 2026-05-11 session 17 during Yoshi's commentary summation. Yoshi: *"one of the features i want with the strongs is a toggle where we can take out all the nikadot and see all words that are the same in a column with their definitions beside them so people can see the truth of the words and the masoretic error. i think thats a must have feature to earmark for the roadmap."* The Masoretic vowel-pointing tradition disambiguated identical consonantal roots into distinct lexical entries by editorial choice; the framework reading is that a portion of those choices obscured connections the unpointed text preserved. The grouped-column view, with nikkudot stripped, makes the consonantal connections visible to any reader without requiring Hebrew. Worked example the framework leans on: *keseh* (כֶּסֶה) for the concealed-moon reading of Psalm 81:3, which shares its consonantal form with *kasah* (כָּסָה) "to cover, conceal" — the Masoretic pointing produces two distinct lexicon entries from one root; the grouped-column view restores the connection.

→ **Locked as Phase-5 flagship feature 2026-05-11.** Earmarked in Section V Phase 5 alongside the basic tap-on-word Strong's lookup. Spec: a reader-side toggle in the Strong's surface. When on, strips Masoretic nikkudot from any lemma in focus and renders a column view of every word in the canon (and extras) that shares the same consonantal form, with its Strong's number and lexicon definition beside it. Sort options (canonical order, frequency, by witness category) TBD at design time. The unpointed-text corpus exists in the eBible USFX source already on disk; the pointed text Yoshi can reach via the existing apparatus on Logos/Accordance/BibleHub for cross-check. Phase-5 wireframe and data-model work to follow the basic tap-on-word lookup ship; final order (which ships first) at Yoshi's pacing call when Phase 5 opens.

**14. Commentary surface — reader toggle, tier-gated.** Surfaced 2026-05-11 session 17 at session-close. Yoshi: *"should also be soemthing that can toggle on and off but the toggle be blocked if not in the right tier so if soemone just wants to read a few chapters, they dont have to have the commentary distracting them."* Two design points: (i) a reader-side toggle to show/hide the framework commentary inline alongside verse text — for partners who want clean reading on a given session without losing the commentary access they're paying for; (ii) the toggle itself is tier-gated — partners below the commentary-bearing tier (per Section III pricing: the $9.99 *Everything + Framework Commentary* tier and above) do not see the toggle, because they don't have commentary access in the first place.

→ **Locked as Phase-4/6/7 cross-cutting feature 2026-05-11.** Spec: (a) UI surface — a small toggle in the reader chrome that hides or shows the inline commentary surface, persisting across chapters as a user preference. **Default state for commentary-tier subscribers: ON.** Commentary is the premium value; show it by default to those paying for it, let them hide it for quick reads. (b) Tier gating — the toggle UI is conditional on the active user tier carrying commentary access. Tiers below the threshold don't see the toggle and don't see the commentary surface at all; the verse-area reflows to full-column reading. (c) Implementation lands across phases: Phase 4 carries the tier-discrimination plumbing already (`tier_satisfies(user_tier, required_tier)` in the schema + JWT-aware `/v1/books` filter); Phase 6 produces the commentary surface itself (verse → concept index + Yoshi-framework-reading-inline render — this is also where the chapter-by-chapter commentary files in `~/Desktop/App/commentaries/<book>-<chapter>.md` flow into the reader); Phase 7 wires the tier-gating to the price-tier definitions so the framework-commentary tier is the one that flips the toggle on. (d) Layout — verse area reflows when commentary is off (full-column verse-only) vs. when commentary is on (verse + commentary aside or inline per the visual design at Phase 6).

Effect on the chapter-by-chapter commentary pass (W-6 in the post-Phase-4 sequence): the per-chapter commentary files produced during the pass are the source of truth for the toggle-on state. The pass produces the data; the reader UI exposes it conditionally. No additional work for the pass beyond what's already named in `~/Desktop/App/commentaries/_COMMENTARY_SUMMATION.md`.

**15. Sacred-names display-preference toggle — back-burner feature idea, surfaced Session 35 (2026-05-12).** Yoshi flagged this mid-session-35 after spotting a competitor app (*Manna*) that ships a Sacred Names settings panel with per-name toggles — *The Father: YAHWEH / YHWH*, *The Son: YESHUA / YAHSHUA*, *The Son's Title: MESSIAH / CHRIST*, *Holy Spirit: DEFAULT / RUACH*. Their name choices are wrong from the voice-skill standard (Yahweh is the inherited four-consonants-with-vowels-from-Adonai mistranslation; the voice skill locks Yahuah per Josephus *Wars* 5.5.7's four-vowel pronunciation; Yeshua is the inherited Strong's spelling; the voice skill locks Yahusha — see Sacred Names Convention block in the skill). But the *mechanic* — give the reader per-name toggles within a settings surface — is worth holding for a later phase.

→ **Held for back-burner consideration, no phase locked yet.** The current convention (sacred names with parentheticals on every mention in body text) is non-negotiable for the default reader experience — that's the retention mechanism that keeps a Christian reader from bouncing off the first paragraph. A future toggle would operate ON TOP of the default, not replace it. Possible options the toggle could surface (decided when this comes off the back burner, voice-gate review required on every option): *Yahuah / Yahuah (God) / God* for the Father with the *(God)* parenthetical as the locked default; *Yahusha / Yahusha (Jesus) / Jesus* for the Son with the *(Jesus)* parenthetical as the locked default; *Messiah / Messiah (Christ) / Christ* for the title with the *(Christ)* parenthetical as the locked default; *Ruach HaKodesh / Ruach HaKodesh (Holy Spirit) / Holy Spirit* for the Spirit with the *(Holy Spirit)* parenthetical as the locked default. **The toggle would never offer *Yahweh* or *Yeshua* as options** — those are voice-skill-rejected. **The toggle's existence and scope is a Phase 6+ decision** and would need a voice-skill update before any of its options surface. Captured here so the idea doesn't get lost; revisit when commentary surface (Phase 6) and reader-side preference architecture are both in design.

**16. Friends-and-family / tester promo codes — should they consume founder slots?** Surfaced Session 37 close (2026-05-12); answered Session 38 open. The question: when Yoshi hands a promo code (FRIENDS_FAMILY or FOUNDER50) to a friend, family member, or tester, does that subscription consume one of the 100 founder slots?

→ **Locked Session 38 open 2026-05-12.** No. Promo-discounted subscriptions bypass the founder-cap claim entirely. The 100-founder cohort stays intact for organic signups; friends/family/tester comp and honorary founder pricing are operationally separate. Webhook detects redemption via Stripe's `total_details.amount_discount` + `discounts` fields, sets `subscriptions.is_promo_subscriber=TRUE`, forces `is_founder_pricing=FALSE`, skips `_claim_founder_slot`. Recorded in `api/subscriptions.py:_handle_checkout_completed`.

**17. Friends-and-family / tester promo codes — duration of the discount?** Surfaced Session 37 close (2026-05-12); answered Session 38 open.

→ **Locked Session 38 open 2026-05-12.** Forever-locked while subscribed. Matches the founder-pricing architecture (the recipient keeps the discount as long as their subscription stays active; cancel + resubscribe later → standard pricing). Stripe Coupons created with `duration=forever`. The user-facing Promotion Code (the string the partner types at checkout) carries `redeem_by` set 90 days out — codes expire on the redemption-window end date, but subscriptions started before the end date keep their forever-locked discount. Yoshi extends or revokes the code anytime in the Stripe dashboard.

**18. Friends-and-family / tester promo codes — unique-vs-generic shape?** Surfaced Session 37 close (2026-05-12); answered Session 38 open + mid-session revision.

→ **Locked Session 38 mid-session 2026-05-12.** Two generic codes, not one. (i) **FRIENDS_FAMILY** — 100% off, forever-locked, generic single-string code Yoshi shares with friends, family, and testers — they get the subscription for free. (ii) **FOUNDER50** — 50% off, forever-locked, generic single-string code for honorary founder partners outside the formal 100-cap mechanism. Both codes managed in the Stripe dashboard (no code-side per-recipient minting). Per-recipient codes (e.g., TESTER_ALICE) can be added as needed via the same Stripe dashboard Coupons → Promotion codes surface with Maximum redemptions=1. Original Session 37-close framing was one shared 50%-off code; Yoshi pivoted at session-38 round-two AskUserQuestion to 100% off, then in conversation added the second 50% founder code. Both transcribed at-the-edge per new lesson (z).

**19. Friends-and-family / tester promo codes — count as partners in dashboard metrics?** Surfaced Session 37 close (2026-05-12); answered Session 38 open.

→ **Locked Session 38 open 2026-05-12.** Yes. Promo-discounted subscriptions ARE counted in the partner-count metric (despite paying $0 or 50% — they're still partners receiving the work). Operationally separate from the founder cohort (no slot consumption) but reportingly equivalent (still a partner). DB column `subscriptions.is_promo_subscriber` is the flag; the partner-count dashboard query (when built) will SUM all rows where status IN ('active','trialing'), and the partial index `idx_subs_promo` lets us cheaply count promo-vs-organic for reporting splits if Yoshi ever wants that breakdown.

**20. Blind-accessibility — first-class support for screen readers, audio-first reading mode, and sacred-name pronunciation in audio.** Surfaced 2026-05-12 mid-Session-38 (Yoshi noted he has a blind friend and asked what could be done to make the app the ultimate Bible for blind partners). A Bible reader is structurally well-suited for blind accessibility because the content is sequential text — the standard accessibility surfaces (screen readers, refreshable Braille displays via Bluetooth, voice navigation via Web Speech API) all work cleanly against a well-structured reading app.

Five layers, ordered by impact:

- **(a) Screen reader compatibility (baseline).** Semantic HTML, ARIA labels on buttons, proper heading hierarchy, focus management, skip-to-content. Free if built right at the React layer. Audit the existing reader + the Session-38 Pricing surface as Phase 9 opens; bake forward into all future surfaces (Manage, commentary, Strong's).

- **(b) Sacred-name pronunciation for audio surfaces — framework-level voice-skill decision.** When a screen reader or TTS engine encounters *Yahuah (God)*, the default voice butchers it ("yuh-HOO-uh"). Three options Yoshi will eventually pick from (or combine):
  - **(b1) Strip parentheticals in audio mode.** *Yahuah (God)* reads as "Yahuah" alone. The parenthetical is a visual reading aid; audio listeners learn the names by repetition the way ancient hearers did.
  - **(b2) Pronunciation lexicon via SSML phoneme hints.** We define the right consonants + vowels once per restored name (Yah-OO-ah for Yahuah, Yah-OO-shah for Yahusha, etc.); every passage benefits across all browsers that support Web Speech SSML.
  - **(b3) Yoshi's voice on the restored names specifically.** The YouTube teaching corpus carries hundreds of hours of Yoshi pronouncing the names. An audio-clip overlay (TTS reads the verse, but inserts Yoshi's voice on every *Yahuah / Yahusha / Yashar'el* etc.) means the partner hears the right pronunciation in the teacher's own voice on every name. Most work to build; most on-mission. The voice skill's *Sacred Names Convention* block will gain an *Audio Pronunciation* sub-section when this is chosen.

- **(c) Audio-first reading mode.** A "Listen to chapter" button that plays the chapter verse-by-verse with TTS — verse-number announcements ("verse twelve"), pause/resume, speed control, bookmark-where-I-stopped, chapter-end auto-advance. Distinct from (a) — (a) is screen-reader-compatible visual UI; (c) is an intentional audio experience for listening rather than reading. Phase 9 wheel.

- **(d) Voice navigation.** "Take me to John three sixteen," "next chapter," "bookmark this verse" via Web Speech recognition. Lighter lift than (c); huge UX win for blind partners who'd otherwise need keyboard shortcuts.

- **(e) Refreshable Braille display support.** Mostly free if (a) is clean. Braille displays connect via Bluetooth and read whatever the screen reader announces. No Braille-specific work required if the screen-reader pass is solid.

→ **Held for Phase 9. Design-partner request open: Yoshi to ask his blind friend whether he'd be willing to be a design partner.** A real blind user testing the app for an hour during Phase 9 will surface more than any accessibility checklist could. He becomes the litmus test for Phase 9 the same way the founder cohort is the launch litmus for partner pricing. If he says yes, his feedback drives the (b1/b2/b3) choice on sacred-name audio pronunciation directly. **Voice-skill update queued** to land when Phase 9 opens: a new *Audio Pronunciation for the Restored Names* sub-section under the Sacred Names Convention, naming whichever of (b1/b2/b3) Yoshi locks in, with worked examples of each restored name and its audio rendering. **Phase plan revision** — Phase 9 currently doesn't exist in Section V; when this is picked up, add *Phase 9 — Accessibility surface: screen readers, audio-first reading, sacred-name pronunciation, voice navigation.*

**21. Tier naming — describe the tooling, not the reader.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. Yoshi floated naming the subscription tiers along a "novice to expert" progression. Voice-skill concern: the series is non-triumphalist — "the remnant is the found, not the elite" — and naming tiers by reader-rank ("novice," "expert") grades the person rather than the content. A decades-walking believer on a lower-priced tier is not a "novice"; "expert" is a word the framework would never apply to a believer. The tiers are levels of study *tooling*, not levels of spiritual maturity.

→ **Locked 2026-05-14.** Yoshi agreed: name the tiers for what they contain (e.g., Reader / Reader + Notes / Reader + Notes + Strong's / Everything + Framework Commentary), never for who the reader is. The Section III five-tier pricing structure is unaffected — this is a naming-layer decision only. Final tier labels set at Phase 7 design.

A reader-side affordance: at the end of a chapter's standard commentary, on chapters carrying a Red Line passage or one of the "usually missed" scriptures (Daniel 7's *kbar enash*, etc.), a "deeper dive" toggle opens the related scriptures for the reader to explore. A repeatable pattern that extends the already-locked Daniel 7:13 end-of-chapter commentary decision (Session 41 close). Must stay thesis-over-prescription — surface the missed scripture and dismantle the inherited reading; never prescribe.

→ **Locked as a Phase-6 feature 2026-05-14.** Distinct from the Phase-6 "Statement of Faith sections as deeper-dive layer" already named — this is a toggle specifically on Red Line / commonly-missed passages. Rides on the same Phase-6 verse→concept index as the commentary surface. Wireframe at Phase 6 design.

**23. All tiers offline + download-choice settings screen.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. (a) Every subscription tier — including free — gets offline capability, not just the paid tiers. (b) On install (or in settings), the user chooses between "download all files for full offline use" and "cached web reader," with a plain-language explanation of the tradeoff. Storage note: the full text corpus (11 editions, ~50,535 verses, Strong's, lexicons, cross-references, commentary) is realistically tens of megabytes — small. The download-choice screen is most meaningful for *audio* (TTS audio, the Phase-9 sacred-name voice clips), which is what can push storage toward the gigabyte range.

→ **Locked 2026-05-14.** All-tiers-offline confirmed as intent. Phase 4 DoD already names "installable to home screen, working offline"; this extends it: (i) offline is not tier-gated; (ii) a download-granularity choice (full-download vs. cached) surfaces at install/in settings, framed primarily around audio. Implementation: Phase 4 service-worker baseline, audio-download choice revisited when Phase 9 audio lands.

Simple data (verse ranges, color, user id, timestamp). "Study notes" is already in the Phase-4 schema list; bookmarks and highlighting are not yet explicit.

→ **Locked as a Phase-4/5 reader feature 2026-05-14.** Schema: add bookmark + highlight tables when the user-content schema work is picked up. UI lands with the reader polish pass (Phase 5-adjacent). Table-stakes for parity with established Bible apps.

**25. Comprehensive notes with related-passage suggestions.** Surfaced 2026-05-14.
