Runs on the *same* Phase-6 verse→concept index that powers the commentary surface — a second consumer of that index, not separate architecture. Must include an off switch: a partner who doesn't want suggestions turns them off in settings and the surface goes quiet (trust-the-reader, same posture as the commentary toggle).

→ **Locked as a Phase-6 feature 2026-05-14.** Depends on the Phase-6 verse→concept index. Default on; one-tap permanent off in settings. No new index architecture — second consumer of the commentary index.

**26. Free-access path for blind partners — coupon mechanism + referral intake.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. Yoshi wants blind partners to have the app free. Decision: not a separate stripped-down tier — a 100%-off, forever-locked coupon applied to the Everything tier (same mechanism as FRIENDS_FAMILY, see Q16–Q18), so a blind partner lands on the *full* Everything tier free. Recommended: a *dedicated* code rather than reusing FRIENDS_FAMILY — keeps the friends/family pool separate, gives clean dashboard reporting on accessibility partners, and can carry an open-ended (or long) redemption window rather than the 90-day FRIENDS_FAMILY window. Paired with a referral/intake flow: a page inviting sighted people to refer blind friends ("if you know someone who is blind, contact us"), routing to Yoshi, who issues the code. Open sub-question: advertise the code openly to blind centers (honor system — consistent with the framework's trust posture) vs. keep it to the referral channel (per-recipient max-1 codes). Both viable; could do both.

→ **Locked as the access mechanism 2026-05-14; phase-aligned with Q20.** The coupon is Stripe-dashboard work (Phase 7-adjacent, doable anytime). Distinct from the *accessibility experience* itself — the five-layer screen-reader / audio / voice-nav build is Q20, Phase 9. The coupon gets blind partners in the door free; Q20 builds what they find there. Honor-system-vs-referral-channel is Yoshi's call.

**27. Framework reading trails.** Surfaced 2026-05-14, Yoshi: "a go." Curated guided journeys through the canon and extras that teach the framework by sequence rather than by lookup — e.g., *The Scattered Seed Trail* (the prophetic gathering passages in order), *The Galatians Re-Read*, *The Judaizer Through-Line*, *The Feasts*. Not generic "read the Bible in a year" plans — framework-shaped paths.

→ **Locked 2026-05-14.** Phase 6-adjacent (trail content draws on the same Teaching Corpus / framework material as the commentary surface). Trail list and sequencing drafted with Yoshi at Phase 6 design.

**28. Multiple public-domain scripture versions + parallel comparison view.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. (a) A downloadable library of scripture versions the reader can choose among. (b) A parallel/side-by-side view — restored-names text beside the KJV base, two editions side by side, or nikkudot-on beside nikkudot-off. On Yoshi's question "how do Bible apps get away with that?": modern translations (NIV, ESV, NASB, etc.) are copyrighted — apps like YouVersion carry hundreds of individual publisher licensing agreements to offer them. The realistic path for this project is the public-domain versions (KJV, ASV 1901, YLT, Webster, Geneva, Douay-Rheims, etc. — all free to use) plus the project's own restored editions.

→ **Locked 2026-05-14 — public-domain versions + restored editions only.** Parallel view locked as a reader feature. Licensing copyrighted modern translations is explicitly out of scope (cost + mission fit); revisitable far-future only. The downloadable library is **operator-curated**, not an open repository — Yoshi controls what is offered; texts outside the framework's discernment (e.g., the Gospel of Thomas and other Gnostic texts) are excluded. Modern *public-domain* options that fit: the World English Bible (incl. its Hebrew Names / Messianic edition) and the Berean Standard Bible.

**36. A More Than Milk Ministries original translation — future aspiration.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. Yoshi's intent: once the apps are generating revenue and Yoshi is supported, produce an original translation under the ministry — owned by the work, not licensed from a publisher. Context (from the Berean Standard Bible discussion): a modern translation is achievable on a far leaner model than the major-publisher machine — it leans on the large body of existing public-domain scholarship (interlinears, lexical data, public-domain critical texts) and modern collaboration tools, and the public-domain-from-day-one status comes from funding it as a gift (private benefaction) rather than a publisher's monetized investment. It is still a serious multi-year undertaking requiring real Hebrew/Greek scholarship, a team, and review — not "easy," but achievable.

→ **Parked as a future aspiration 2026-05-14 — explicitly sequenced *after* the apps are selling and Yoshi is supported.** Not a current-phase item. The sequencing — sustaining revenue and support first, then the translation — is the sound order and is recorded as Yoshi's own framing. Revisit when the app is generating sustaining revenue.

**37. Scriptural maps — historical, modern, and ancient→modern place mapping.** Surfaced 2026-05-14. A maps feature for the reader: (a) **historical maps** surfaced on the chapters that reference them — e.g., older cosmographical maps (the north-pole mountain surrounded by four islands) on passages like "the sides of the north" (Isaiah 14:13; Psalm 48:2) — each with an explanation; (b) **modern maps and site photography** from public-domain sources; (c) **ancient→modern place mapping** — where Persia, Idumea, and other named places sit on the modern map. All map assets are **self-hosted** — downloaded and served from the project's own infrastructure rather than hot-linked to external sites — so the feature can't break from link rot or an external host going down. (Accuracy note: public-domain *status* can't be revoked once a work is public domain; the real risk self-hosting guards against is a specific external scan/host becoming unavailable — so the instinct is sound, and it applies to *all* the app's public-domain assets, not just maps.)

→ **Locked as a feature, phased v2+ 2026-05-14.** Yoshi: most of this lands in v2 and beyond, not the v1 launch. Feature decision recorded here; the map *asset files* live in the `source-texts/` area when the feature is built. Phase placement: post-launch (v2+), Phase 6-adjacent or its own later phase.

**Session 46 working record — tier restructure (2026-05-14).** Discussion wheel. Decisions landed across the session, in order:

- **Free tier — SETTLED.** Free carries: the restored Protestant 66; the **full public-domain reference apparatus** — lexicons (Brown-Driver-Briggs, Thayer's Greek, Gesenius), concordances, Treasury of Scripture Knowledge cross-references, classic public-domain Bible dictionaries (Easton's, Smith's, ISBE 1915), maps and atlases — **operator-curated per the Q28 principle**; **Strong's integration**; **cross-references**; **one highlight color**; **no notes** (all note-taking is paid — Yoshi's decision; the plain-note-free / smart-notes-paid split was offered and declined). Carried unchanged from Section III (not contradicted this session): Statement of Faith doorway (I–VII), daily reading plan, basic reader, search across the free canon, offline (per Q23, all-tiers-offline). **Free-tier logic settled:** free's "leg up" is *the restored text + the full public-domain apparatus* — the apparatus is precisely what Logos gates behind Word Study and competitors price at the $9.99 band, so giving it away free is what puts free "in line with their paid versions" (Yoshi's stated bar). Memorization/export placement (Q40b judgment call 1) settled: both at $1.99 (see below).
- **Public-domain commentaries — EXCLUDED, project-wide content boundary (Yoshi's decision).** Public-domain commentaries (Matthew Henry, Jamieson-Fausset-Brown, Barnes' Notes, Gill, Clarke, Calvin, etc.) are **not bundled into the app at any tier.** They carry the inherited antinomian / replacement reading wall-to-wall; including them would seat that reading inside the app beside the restored text (Red Line #2). The Q28 operator-curation principle ("texts outside the framework's discernment excluded") extends to the public-domain tools library: reference apparatus in, commentaries out.
- **Yoshi's working tier sketch — base shape settled, further work remaining.** $1.99 = all of free + the Apocrypha (14 books) + basic notes + memorization + export (see notes and memorization/export decisions below). $4.99 = all extra books of scripture (~50 beyond canon) + multiple highlight colors + bookmark organize/label + smart-notes system + all *already-existing* commentary (every extra-canonical book, plus Romans 11, Galatians, Daniel 7) + the dismantling units (see below). $9.99 / $14.99 set aside as future tiers (carry full canon framework commentary when Beta v2 finishes it, plus live sermons / courses / Teaching Corpus). Remaining open: beta-day-one tier set still to be pressure-tested; the full canon framework commentary tier home (likely $9.99) not yet formally locked.
- **Notes — start at $1.99 (Yoshi's decision, locked).** All note-taking is paid. Free carries no notes. $1.99 is where a partner first touches the text as their own working document. Yoshi explicitly declined the plain-note-free / smart-notes-paid split that was offered. Notes at $1.99 is the harder, cleaner line and it is logged as Yoshi's.
- **Memorization and export — $1.99 (settled with notes).** Both land at $1.99 alongside basic notes. The Q40b judgment-call-1 (memorization/export free vs. $1.99) is closed: $1.99. Role of the $1.99 tier: free is where you read and study with the full apparatus; $1.99 is where you start working the text yourself. *(The slogan name "make it yours" floated here in Session 46 was rejected by Yoshi in Session 47 — the tier is named "Notes" per Q21. See the Session 47 working record.)*
- **Free study guides on the kingdom gospel — included in free (Yoshi's decision).** A few bounded study guides on the kingdom gospel go into the free tier. The Free Truth Principle: the doorway gospel is free, and the kingdom gospel *is* the doorway — so guided study of it is not a giveaway, it is the doorway made walkable. Also resolves the gap where free carried text + tools but no teaching surface at all. Bounded to the kingdom gospel at this stage; does not collide with the Q39 beta-defer commentary plan. Material exists — the kingdom gospel is the spine of everything already written.
- **Three content categories — locked (Yoshi's decision).** (1) **Reference apparatus: in (free and up)** — public-domain lexicons, dictionaries, concordances, maps, cross-references, operator-curated per Q28. (2) **Public-domain commentaries as neutral reference: out (all tiers)** — Matthew Henry, JFB, Barnes', Gill, Clarke, Calvin, etc. carry the inherited reading wall-to-wall; bundling them neutrally violates Red Line #2. (3) **Public-domain works as dismantling targets: in (higher tiers, $4.99 and up)** — the framework takes these works apart; quoting and excerpting them for teardown is a categorically different use than neutral reference. Not a contradiction of (2); the distinction the voice skill itself draws.
- **Dismantling units — excerpt-only, public-domain targets only (Yoshi's decision, locked).** Dismantling units quote and excerpt only the passages being addressed; the full work never lives in the app. The reader never gets a clean copy of the inherited reading to wander off with — the teardown quotes what it needs and takes it apart. Targets are public-domain works only (not copyrighted Logos-licensed material). Yoshi's read — "quoting for teardown is likely fair use" for copyrighted works — is recorded as his *stated view pending legal confirmation*, not settled policy (per Q44 instruction). The public-domain route takes the legal risk to near zero and covers the same payload, because it is the *reading* being dismantled, not any one author's prose. The copyrighted-works question routes to an IP attorney as part of Q41 (licensing research); build the feature now on public-domain ground.
- **Naming — confirmed, no change (Yoshi's decision).** *The Remnant of Promise Official Study Bible* stands. The "of the Remnant of Promise" construction was raised and declined: longer, awkward on the spine and in App Store name fields, and edges toward a triumphalist institutional read the framework's non-triumphalist posture (the remnant is the found, not the elite) does not support. Section IV naming lock unchanged.

**44. Process instruction from Yoshi (2026-05-14) — file-and-review is for content, not for rules.** Recorded here in Yoshi's words' substance, for his confirmation: making things into files for Yoshi to review-and-agree-to is the *right* tool for **content deliverables** (study notes, chapters — things Yoshi knows to go read) and the *wrong* tool for **assumptions and rules**. A rule or policy quietly written into an authoritative file becomes the project's de-facto policy whether or not Yoshi ratified it — which is exactly how the disputed Section III AI-policy clause came to sit in "Locked Decisions." The line: Claude **records Yoshi's decisions** and **executes the work** under the existing judgment-and-land discipline, but Claude does **not author the project's governing rules/policies/assumptions and file them as "settled."** Rules and policies require Yoshi's explicit ratification.

**45. Website — nav "Log In" link broken. RESOLVED Session 45.** Surfaced 2026-05-14 Session 44, investigating Yoshi's report that the "Log In" link gives logged-out visitors a "no such page." **Session 44's stated cause — "WordPress's automatic page-list fallback" with a "theme/LoginWP-generated" Log In link — was not accurate.** Session 45 read the live theme: the site's top nav is rendered by `rop_render_primary_nav()` in the live `functions.php`, a purpose-built custom nav function (hardcoded page list with active-page highlighting, a BuddyPress "Community" item that switches on login state, a logged-out "Log In" / "Try Our Own Social Platform" pair, and a separate member-area nav strip). It is the *intended* navigation, not a crude fallback, and it runs because no menu is assigned to the Primary location. The live `functions.php` is 184 lines — well past the 110-line May-7 deploy copy in the local mirror. The actual bug was one line: the logged-out "Log In" link used `wp_login_url()`, which returns `/wp-login.php`; the site's security plugin renamed login to `/goshen/` and 404s `wp-login.php`. **Fix (Yoshi's call, Session 45): the one-line surgical edit, not the menu rebuild** — building and assigning a static Primary Menu would have made the theme run `wp_nav_menu()` *instead of* `rop_render_primary_nav()`, destroying the dynamic login-state switching to fix a one-line bug. Changed line 117 `wp_login_url()` → `home_url( '/goshen/' )` (matching the Register link on the next line) via the WP-admin Theme File Editor; WP Engine cache purged; verified live in the logged-out view — "Log In" → `/goshen/` (HTTP 200, working WordPress login form). The original Q45 framing (build + assign a Primary Menu) is **not** done and is **not** needed. See the Section X session-45 entry, including the divergence note on the stale local WP theme mirror.

The site's top nav is rendered by `rop_render_primary_nav()` in the live `functions.php` — a purpose-built custom nav function: the hardcoded page list with active-page highlighting, a BuddyPress "Community" item that switches on `is_user_logged_in()`, a logged-out "Log In" / "Try Our Own Social Platform" pair, and a separate `rop_render_member_nav()` strip for logged-in users. **(b) The actual bug — one line.** Line 117, the logged-out "Log In" link, used `esc_url( wp_login_url() )`. `wp_login_url()` returns `/wp-login.php`; the site's security plugin renamed login to `/goshen/` and redirects `wp-login.php` → `/404/`. So logged-out visitors hit "no such page." The Register link on the very next line already did it right (`home_url( '/register/' )`), and Log Out worked — only the Log In href was never updated to the renamed URL.

**(c) Fix approach — Yoshi's call.** Surfaced both options to Yoshi via AskUserQuestion: the one-line surgical edit, or the Session-44-handoff plan of building a static Primary Menu and assigning it. Building and assigning a menu makes the theme run `wp_nav_menu()` *instead of* `rop_render_primary_nav()` — which would have thrown away the dynamic logged-in/out switching and the BuddyPress integration to fix a one-line bug. Yoshi chose the surgical edit.

**(d) The edit, live.** Changed line 117 `wp_login_url()` → `home_url( '/goshen/' )` in the live `functions.php` via the WP-admin Theme File Editor (CodeMirror — the value was set through the CodeMirror API, not the raw textarea, so the form submit carried the change). `wp_login_url()` appeared exactly once in the file, so no risk of a wrong-target replace. WordPress's loopback check passed — "File edited successfully," no fatal error. WP Engine cache purged via the admin-bar Quick Links → "Quick clear all cache."

**(e) Verified live, logged-out.** Fetched the homepage and `/goshen/` with credentials omitted (true logged-out view): the nav "Log In" link now resolves to `https://remnantofpromise.org/goshen/` (no `wp-login` anywhere in it); `/goshen/` returns HTTP 200 with a working WordPress login form (`#loginform`, user + password fields, title "Log In ‹ Remnant of Promise"). **2026-05-14 — Session 42 (Yoshi + Claude). WP companion plugin v2 — `bible-companion` HMAC partner-tier endpoint landed in source + live-verified on the WordPress side.** New single-file PHP plugin at tracked `wp-companion/bible-companion/bible-companion.php` (~370 lines, v1.1.0), the second plugin in the `wp-companion/` folder Session 41 opened. Exposes `POST /wp-json/rop/v1/partner-tier` with HMAC-SHA256 auth via `X-ROP-Signature` + `X-ROP-Timestamp` headers (signing input `"<ts>.<raw_body>"`, ±300s replay-tolerance window matching Stripe's webhook convention). Body `{wp_user_id: int, partner_tier: string}`; the plugin validates `wp_user_id` is a positive int and `partner_tier` is one of the five-value whitelist (`free|study_notes|extras|complete_study|everything`, matching `api/auth.py::PartnerTier`), then writes the `rop_partner_tier` user-meta key — and *only* that key, so the shared secret carries no admin scope. API-side: `_sync_partner_tier_to_wp` in `api/subscriptions.py` refactored into a path-selector — `WP_COMPANION_SECRET` set → new HMAC path (`_sync_partner_tier_via_companion` + `_sign_companion_request` helper); unset but `WP_API_*` set → Session 37 Application Password path (`_sync_partner_tier_via_app_password`, preserved as graceful fallback through the Session 42→43 transition); both unset → log-and-skip (existing shape). New `wp_companion_secret` field in `api/config.py`; new `WP_COMPANION_SECRET` env var in `hosting/render.yaml` (`sync: false`). API version bumped `0.6.0-phase4-session40` → `0.7.0-phase4-session42`. **Removes the Session 37 admin Application Password dependency** — cleaner security posture, no broad-scope credential sitting in Render env vars long-term. **42/42 sandbox smoke checks PASS** (`_scratch/_session42_smoke_test.py` — Python mirror of the PHP verifier as the executable contract spec; round-trips the API signer through it; exercises path selection + HTTP-outcome handling with a fake httpx client); **Session 41's 22/22 + Session 40's 18/18 regression smoke also PASS** — no breaks. Session opened against the Session 41 close handoff (Section XI), which named the wheel as *WP companion plugin v2: `bible-companion` HMAC plugin*. Voice skill loaded fresh (five markers verified: Operating Disciplines + Melek Tsadiq + ⚠ WARNING TO CLAUDE + layered Red Line #11 + Red Line #12 Son of Adam).

**(d) Architecture decisions (apply-judgment-land-the-work, no per-line gates per handoff discipline g).** HMAC-SHA256 (matches `rop-sso-bridge`'s sha256 + the Session 41 NEW-open default). Header pair `X-ROP-Signature` (hex digest) + `X-ROP-Timestamp` (Unix seconds, ±300s tolerance) — Stripe's pattern, gives replay protection. Signing input `"<ts>.<raw_body>"`. Plugin slug `bible-companion`, author `Yoshi` (Session 41 precedent). API env var `WP_COMPANION_SECRET`. Fallback ladder preserves the Session 37 Application Password path so the rollout is graceful, not a hard cutover.

**(e) Secret-storage decision changed mid-install — verbal answer transcribed per lesson (z).** The plugin was first written (v1.0.0) to read the shared secret from a `ROP_COMPANION_SECRET` constant in `wp-config.php`, mirroring `rop-sso-bridge`'s read of `JWT_AUTH_SECRET_KEY`. During the install walkthrough Yoshi couldn't locate `wp-config.php` (WP Engine doesn't surface it in the WP admin dashboard — it's a server file). AskUserQuestion offered (a) add a paste-it-here WP admin settings page vs (b) reach `wp-config.php` via WP Engine SFTP/File Manager. **Yoshi's decision: (a) — the admin settings page.** Plugin reworked to v1.1.0: reads the secret via `rop_companion_get_secret()`, which checks the `ROP_COMPANION_SECRET` constant first (kept as an optional advanced-operator override) and falls back to a `rop_companion_secret` WordPress option set on a new **Settings → Bible Companion** admin page (WP Settings API, `manage_options` cap, password field, never echoes the saved value back into page HTML, blank-submission-keeps-existing sanitize callback). Security posture: the option is scoped to this one plugin only — still a categorical improvement over the admin-wide Application Password. The HMAC verification contract itself did not change, so the 42/42 smoke (which passes the secret as a parameter) held across the rework.

**(f) Live verification — WordPress side confirmed end-to-end via direct HMAC curl test.** Rather than route verification through a live Stripe event (heavier; touches a real subscription row), the plugin endpoint was tested directly: a self-contained macOS `openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac` one-liner builds a fresh-timestamped signed request each run. Sandbox pre-check confirmed the openssl one-liner produces a byte-identical signature to the API's `_sign_companion_request`. Yoshi ran it against `https://remnantofpromise.org/wp-json/rop/v1/partner-tier` with a deliberately-bogus `wp_user_id: 999999` (zero risk — no real row touched). First run returned `{"ok":false,"error":"invalid signature"}` — diagnosed as a paste discrepancy in the secret saved on the Settings page; Yoshi re-saved the secret cleanly; second run returned **`{"ok":false,"error":"user not found"}`** — the pass signal. That response proves the full security gate end-to-end: plugin installed + active, REST route registered, the secret on the WP Settings page matches the test key, signature verified, timestamp inside the tolerance window, body parsed + validated — the only step that "failed" is the deliberately-fake user lookup. The WordPress half of Session 42 is live-verified.

**(e) Repair shipped: `rop-sso-bridge` plugin.** Single-file PHP at `wp-companion/rop-sso-bridge/rop-sso-bridge.php` (also at `_scratch/_session41_wp_sso_bridge/rop-sso-bridge.php` as the working scratch + `~/Desktop/App/rop-sso-bridge.zip` as upload-ready artifact). Three hooks: (i) `wp_login` action — mints HS256 JWT (header `{typ:JWT, alg:HS256}`, payload `{iss: home_url(), iat, nbf, exp: iat+7d, data.user.{id, login, display_name, partner_tier}}` exactly matching `api/auth.py::_decode_token`'s contract), sets cookie at `Domain=.remnantofpromise.org; Path=/; Secure=true; HttpOnly=false; SameSite=Lax`. (ii) `wp_logout` action — clears the same-named cookie with same domain/path attributes. (iii) `admin_init` action — refresh-if-missing (catches the case where the 7-day cookie expired but the WP auth cookie hadn't; benign, only fires when cookie absent + headers-not-sent). Self-contained (no external PHP dependencies — native `hash_hmac` + `base64_encode` + `wp_json_encode`); failure modes all log + bail (missing `JWT_AUTH_SECRET_KEY` → log + skip; setcookie returns false → log + continue; user-meta unset → defaults to `'free'`); never breaks WP login itself.

**New operating lessons logged (NEW Session 41):**
- **(ff) Trace the cookie-issuance path upstream first when an auth cookie is missing or rejected.** When diagnosing an auth-cookie failure, don't start with claim-level diagnoses (expiry, signature, iss mismatch, key drift). Start with: *what code is supposed to set this cookie, and is it running?* The Session 40 close addendum named three priority diagnoses — (a) expired exp, (b) signing-key drift, (c) cookie not being set at all. The Session 41 wheel converged on (c) within five minutes of cookie-sweep evidence — but only because the diagnostic step asked the right question early. Had the diagnostic chased (a) or (b) first (decode + inspect exp + compare against current time + test alternate secrets), it would have wasted the session because there was no cookie to decode in the first place. **Rule:** in any auth-cookie debug, run the cookie-sweep BEFORE any decode work. If no cookie exists, the diagnosis is "what should set this and isn't" — a code/architecture question, not a claims question.
- **(gg) File handover lands at a path the operator can find without instructions.** When presenting a deliverable file to Yoshi for him to upload/install elsewhere, the file's on-disk path matters as much as the chat card. Files buried in `_scratch/_session41_wp_sso_bridge/` or in the outputs folder under `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/...` look the same in chat cards but are not equally findable in Finder. The operator's path of least resistance is to navigate via Finder to a known top-level location (the App folder, the Desktop). **Rule:** when a session produces a file Yoshi has to upload to a third-party system (WP plugin upload, Stripe dashboard CSV, Render env file, etc.), drop a copy at `~/Desktop/App/<filename>` — the top of his project folder — even if the canonical lives elsewhere. The chat card is supplementary; the Finder-discoverable on-disk path is the canonical handoff. Cleanup of those temp drops happens at session close per the operator-side wrap walkthrough.

**Mid-session-end voice/translation note from Yoshi (added at session close per lesson z, transcribed verbatim from his Facebook reply to a public commenter's question on the post linked at the FEEDBACK ASK).** Commenter Isaiah AndAnja-Brielle Chavez asked: *"Question for your study bible. You mentioned using 'Son of Adam' everywhere 'Son of Man' was flattened. Do you also do this in Daniel when its says: כְּבַר אֱנָשׁ ?"* — naming Daniel 7:13's Aramaic *k'bar enash* (the comparative *k* + *bar* (son) + *enash* (man) — the famous "one like a son of man" coming with the clouds of heaven). Yoshi's answer: *"Hey brother, yes I will be translating it as Son of Adam and listing in the notes the use of enash Aramaic man, and pointing out that the writer obviously knew the term son of man and copied it back as the Aramaic word, having no understanding of what the term meant. which is why we have Daniel 6 in Hebrew and the oldest fully preserved Daniel 7 is Aramaic from after messiah died. 1008 ce, well into the christianized term. and I will add that to the notes. in the end of chapter commentary."* **Editorial decision locked**: Daniel 7:13 renders as *Son of Adam* consistent with Red Line #12, with end-of-chapter commentary footnoting (a) the Aramaic *enash* in the source text, (b) the textual history that the oldest fully preserved Aramaic Daniel 7 dates to 1008 CE — post-Christ, well into the period of Christianized term drift, and (c) the framing that the Aramaic translator/scribe copied back the *son of Adam* concept as Aramaic *enash* without understanding the lineage weight the original phrase named. **Voice-skill update queue gains a fifth item** alongside the four operating-discipline lessons (dd/ee/ff/gg): a Red Line #12 addendum paragraph carrying the Aramaic *bar enash* nuance + the Daniel 7 textual-history framing, so any future commentary work on Daniel — and any future voice-skill reader — has the same framing baked in. This is voice-content, not operating-discipline; lands in the next voice-skill rebuild's Red Line #12 as an addendum paragraph. **No immediate code/Bible-app change needed** — the corpus already renders Daniel 7:13 with whatever the source text carries; the commentary surface where this footnote lives is Phase 6 (commentary toggle, tier-gated per Q14). The framing is queued so it's there when Phase 6 opens.

**(e) Period_end extraction fix.** New helper `_extract_period_end(stripe_sub) -> Optional[int]` in `api/subscriptions.py` (~55 lines, between `_stripe_client()` and the `_upsert_user` DB helper block, under a new `# ---- Stripe object readers ----` section header). Walks the deprecated top-level `current_period_end` first (legacy API pins still populate it), falls back to `subscription.items.data[0].current_period_end` (the post-acacia path on single-item subscriptions — our model). Accepts either a `stripe.Subscription` SDK object or a plain dict (webhook event payloads are JSON-parsed into dicts); internal `_read` closure handles dict-style, `.get()`-style, and `getattr`-style access uniformly. Returns `None` on any unreadable shape — non-fatal upstream (`cancel_subscription` falls through to the no-period-end UPDATE branch, `_handle_subscription_change` passes None into the existing `COALESCE(to_timestamp($3), current_period_end)` which preserves the prior row value). Two call sites updated: `_handle_subscription_change` line 676 (`obj.get("current_period_end")` → `_extract_period_end(obj)`); `cancel_subscription` lines 959–964 (the four-line `try/except getattr` block → one-line `_extract_period_end(stripe_sub)`).

**Operator-side deploy addendum (post-source-close, 2026-05-13).** Live deploy ran through end-to-end on Yoshi's side following the chat walkthrough. Six items surfaced and were either fixed in-session or logged for Session 41. **(i)** **render.yaml `region: oregon` on a static site was rejected by the Blueprint validator** with `services[1] static sites cannot have a region`. Static sites serve from Render's global CDN edge, not a single region; only Web Services and Postgres carry `region`. One-line fix in `hosting/render.yaml` (removed the line, added a 4-line comment explaining why). Separate commit `5fb4534` ("Session 40 fix: remove region from PWA static-site service"), Render Blueprint re-sync went green, new `remnant-app-pwa` Static Site service created and first build landed (3-5 min `cd app && npm install && npm run build`, npm installed the Linux-native `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu` cleanly on Render's build env vs the sandbox's macOS-host-mount inability to load it). **(ii)** **Sandbox-side git operations left a stale `.git/index.lock` on disk that blocked Yoshi's first commit attempt** with `fatal: Unable to create '.git/index.lock': File exists`. Lesson (i) from Session 39 surfaced again — sharper version locked: any session where my Edit/Read/Write tools touch files in a git-versioned mount, expect that operator's first `git add` after will fail with the index.lock error, recoverable via `rm .git/index.lock` from the Mac terminal (safe to run any time when no real git process is active locally). To be folded into the operator-discipline rules at next voice-skill rebuild as lesson **(dd)**. **(iii)** **DNS-host detour cost a few minutes**: initial guess of WP Engine for DNS (based on Session 36 install file naming WP Engine as the WP host) was wrong — WP Engine's "Point DNS" page made clear they're not the DNS provider, just consume the records you set elsewhere. Subsequent guess of Cloudflare (based on the `_cf-custom-hostname` TXT record pattern) also wrong. Ground truth from `dig NS remnantofpromise.org +short` returned `*.wixdns.net` — Wix is both the registrar AND the DNS host. The Wix "Your domain is set to point away from Wix" warning is misleading (means the domain isn't assigned to a Wix-hosted website, NOT that DNS is delegated elsewhere). Lesson: **`dig NS` is the always-right first step on any DNS routing question** — faster than searching install docs or pattern-matching on TXT records. To be folded as lesson **(ee)**. **(iv)** **Subdomain split landed live**: Wix DNS got two CNAMEs (`bible.remnantofpromise.org` → `remnant-app-pwa.onrender.com`, `api.bible.remnantofpromise.org` → `remnant-app-api.onrender.com`, both 1 Hour TTL). Render dashboard custom-domain swap: removed `bible.` from `remnant-app-api`, added `api.bible.` to `remnant-app-api`, added `bible.` to `remnant-app-pwa`. Both Let's Encrypt certs issued green within ~2 minutes. **(v)** **Live URL verification 4/4 PASS on the deploy itself**: `curl https://api.bible.remnantofpromise.org/` returned `"version":"0.6.0-phase4-session40"`; `/v1/health` returned `status:ok, db_reachable:true, schema_version:"1.0.0-phase4-session38"`; `bible.remnantofpromise.org/` loaded the Reader chrome with the anonymous-tier 66-book canon rendering correctly (no "Books failed to load" red banner, footer shows `Live from api.bible.remnantofpromise.org/v1` correctly); dig chain clean (`bible.` → `remnant-app-pwa.onrender.com` → Render's GCP origin → Cloudflare CDN; `api.bible.` likewise). **(vi)** **JWT auth flow on production showed up as a pre-existing latent issue — headline Session 41 open.** With the PWA on `bible.remnantofpromise.org` for the first real-UX test, Manage.tsx rendered the **anonymous "Sign in at remnantofpromise.org"** view state despite Yoshi being signed in to WordPress (the WP admin bar showed "Howdy, assemblyofrop"). Diagnostic: Safari Web Inspector confirmed `rop_jwt` cookie exists at `Domain=.remnantofpromise.org` (leading dot — correct for cross-subdomain visibility). Wire shape on both ends is verified correct (PWA `api.ts` sends `Authorization: Bearer <cookie-value>` from `readJwtCookie()` AND `credentials: 'include'` on every fetch; API `auth.py::get_current_user_optional` reads BOTH the `rop_jwt` cookie via FastAPI `Cookie(...)` AND the `Authorization` header). The cookie travels but the API still treats the caller as anonymous — meaning the JWT value is being rejected by the decode step. Most likely diagnoses: (a) the `exp` claim is in the past (expired token, WP plugin not refreshing on session activity); (b) signing-key drift between WP's `JWT_AUTH_SECRET_KEY` in `wp-config.php` and Render's `JWT_SECRET` env var; (c) the cookie was set during Session 36/37 with a now-stale value and never gets re-issued on subsequent WP logins. **Not a Session 40 regression** — same auth-cookie wiring concern existed pre-Session-40, was being papered over by Session 39's dev-only `?dev_jwt=` localhost handler. **Session 41's recommended wheel.** **(vii)** **WP login URL friction surfaced when Yoshi tried to log out + back in** to refresh the JWT cookie — `/login` returns 404 (no friendly slug on his WP install), and Yoshi reported `/wp-login.php` also broken at the moment. Standard recovery paths: `/wp-admin/` (canonical admin entry), `/wp-login.php` (default login), WP Engine User Portal's password-reset button if both fail. Operator friction I caused by telling him to log out before confirming a working re-login URL. Logged for Session 41's auth-debug start: confirm the active login URL on his WP install (search Plugins → WPS Hide Login or similar), then continue the JWT cookie diagnostic.

No migration needed for this wheel. Webhook's `_handle_subscription_change` (Session 37) already syncs `cancel_at_period_end` back from Stripe's `customer.subscription.updated` event — the new endpoint just adds an immediate-flip path on the partner's side without disrupting the Stripe-side reconciliation already in place.

**(e) API surface — new `POST /v1/subscriptions/cancel`.** Mounted at the existing prefix; JWT-gated via `get_current_user_required` (Session 37's wrapper). The handler reads the partner's most-recent subscription row by `wordpress_user_id`; returns 404 if none exists, 409 if the row has no `stripe_subscription_id` (corrupt-state guard), 200 idempotent-no-op if the row is already in a terminal state (`canceled`/`unpaid`/`incomplete_expired`) or already carries `cancel_at_period_end=TRUE`. Otherwise calls `stripe.Subscription.modify(stripe_subscription_id, cancel_at_period_end=True)`, mirrors Stripe's authoritative `current_period_end` onto the local row via `to_timestamp($1)`, re-reads the row, and returns the full state to the front-end. 502 on Stripe-side `StripeError` with the error's `user_message` (falls back to `str(e)` when user_message is None — modern SDK exposes it as a read-only property). New `CancelResponse` Pydantic model mirrors `SubscriptionMeResponse`'s shape so Manage.tsx can render directly from the response without a `/me` re-fetch.

You can resubscribe anytime. Your {$X} price is locked to you and stays the same if you come back") + Confirm/Keep buttons, (e) just-cancelled → emerald banner with the same copy. Dollar formatting handles 0 → "$0", whole dollars → "$75", cents → "$74.50". Date formatting via `toLocaleDateString` with year/month/day. No "are you sure" anywhere. The button between Confirm-cancellation and Keep-partnership is the only choice surfaced — no third-option upsell, no retention pitch.

**New operating lessons logged (NEW session 38):**
- **(z) Verbal mid-session adds get transcribed at-the-edge, not batched at close.** Yoshi's "oh i want a 50% founders code too, but i wanted the friend and family to be free" arrived after the AskUserQuestion-with-recommendation-first round had already locked one promo code. The right move (taken) was to immediately fold the second code into the operator file + the smoke test + this Section X entry as the work proceeded — not to defer it to close as a single batched transcription. Session-end is for *verifying* every verbal answer landed; the landing itself should happen as the answer arrives, while context is fresh. Lesson w (verbal-answers-transcribe) was the at-close discipline; lesson z sharpens it: *at-the-edge, not at-the-close*. Especially important when the verbal answer materially changes the deliverable shape (here, doubling the operator file's coupon section + adding a second smoke test case).
- **(aa) Render Postgres requires SSL on every connection AND Apple's bundled Python's LibreSSL doesn't trust Render's CA chain — pass an explicit `ssl.SSLContext` with `verify_mode=CERT_NONE` to every `asyncpg.connect()` call.** Surfaced as a two-stage failure during Yoshi's first Session 38 apply. Stage 1: bare URL → `InvalidAuthorizationSpecificationError: SSL/TLS required` (Render hardened plaintext rejection between Session 35 — when the apply ran clean — and Session 38). Stage 2: with `ssl='require'` string kwarg patched in → `ConnectionDoesNotExistError: connection was closed in the middle of operation` (SSL handshake started but failed mid-stream because Apple's LibreSSL system cert store doesn't include Render's CA). Stage 3 fix that landed cleanly: pass an explicit `ssl.SSLContext()` with `check_hostname=False` + `verify_mode=ssl.CERT_NONE`. This does encryption-in-transit (which is all Render requires) without identity verification (which we don't need — Render's hostname is hard-coded in `_db_url.txt`, not user-typed, not vulnerable to spoofing). **Rule for future scripts touching the live Render Postgres:** define a `_make_ssl_context()` helper that returns an SSLContext with verification off, and pass it as the `ssl=` kwarg to every `asyncpg.connect(...)` call. Both Session 38 scripts (`_session38_apply.py` + `_session38_tier_pricing_seed.py`) carry the helper; Session 39+ scripts copy the four-line pattern. (For psql-based runs that ever surface in future operator files: `?sslmode=require` in the URL works because psql uses the system OpenSSL with the right CA bundle, not LibreSSL.)
- **(bb) `>>` against a file with no trailing newline appends without breaking the previous line.** Surfaced when Yoshi was given an `echo "?sslmode=require" >> _db_url.txt` workaround mid-recovery from lesson (aa) stage 1. `echo` adds a trailing newline; `printf "%s"` doesn't. The two appends produced a three-line file from a one-line URL, breaking the asyncpg URL parse. **Rule:** any single-line-content file Claude has the operator append to gets a explicit reset path documented at the same time as the append command — or better, just have the operator open the file in TextEdit and edit-in-place rather than shell-redirect-append. The reset path that worked was `sed -n '1p' ... | sed 's/?sslmode=.*$//' > tmp && mv tmp original`. Cleaner forward path: never have the operator append to a config file from the shell; always edit-in-place.

- **(cc) Walkthrough content stays IN CHAT, not in operator files. Files are for things that must live on disk (secrets, code, KV inputs the script reads). Sequential click-by-click instructions go directly in the chat response.** Locked Session 38 2026-05-12 at Yoshi's explicit direction: *"make it a hard note, dont ever save me a follow click by click instruction guide. give me the instructions here it will save time, dont forget im autistic and have disabilities."* Context-switching between chat and a separate markdown file to read a walkthrough is friction Yoshi specifically should not have to absorb — autism + disability make the context-switch costlier than the file-based discipline saves. The original file-based discipline (Session 35 lesson t) was about not piping secrets through `pbpaste`; I conflated "secrets go in files" with "instructions go in files" and that was wrong. **Rule for every future Claude session with Yoshi:** when a dashboard walkthrough, click-by-click sequence, or sequential operator path is needed, write it directly in the chat response. The chat is the surface Yoshi reads as he works. Files still hold: (i) secrets and connection strings (`_db_url.txt`, `_jwt_secret.txt`), (ii) KV inputs scripts read programmatically (`_session38_stripe_price_ids.txt`), (iii) executable code (`.py`, `.sh`, `.sql`), (iv) the project roadmap itself. Files do NOT hold the prose walkthrough of *how to use* any of the above. That goes in chat. The operator-onboarding-markdown pattern from Session 37 (`_session37_stripe_setup.md`) is **deprecated for new work** — keep the existing ones as historical record, but stop writing new ones. The roadmap's Section X session-38 entry's mention of `_session38_stripe_pricing_expansion.md` is the last instance; future sessions surface the same content as chat responses instead. **Voice-skill bundle queue gains this rule** for the next rebuild.

All four answered at session open and folded into the operator file + the webhook detection logic.

**(d) Schema inventory before writing the dependency.** Read `data-schema/schema.sql` lines 375–488 — the tables relevant to this wheel: `users` (UUID PK, `wordpress_user_id` UNIQUE — the lazy-upsert join key with WP), `subscription_tier_prices` (the catalog the handoff colloquially called "tier_pricing" — actual table name; UNIQUE on `(tier, cadence, is_founder_pricing)`), `subscriptions` (the row written on checkout completion; `locked_price_cents` carries the price-lock per Section III), `founder_pricing_counter` (single-row, default cap=1000, `CHECK (id = 1)` — SELECT FOR UPDATE pattern named in the schema's own COMMENT), `purchase_records` (`stripe_event_id UNIQUE` is the schema-side webhook idempotency mechanism). Existing types: `subscription_status` enum mirrors Stripe's lifecycle 1:1; `billing_cadence` enum has `monthly` + `annual`; `purchase_kind` has no `failure` value (we use `subscription_payment` + `notes='payment_failed'` for invoice.payment_failed events).

All default to empty string — empty default = the corresponding feature degrades gracefully (checkout → 503 with operator-pointing message, webhook → 503, WP-sync → log-and-skip). `extra="ignore"` already on the model so unrecognized env keys don't trip startup.

**(i) New file `api/subscriptions.py`.** ~520 lines. Three endpoints + helpers + a `get_current_user_required` dependency (wraps Session 36's `get_current_user_optional`, raises 401 when None). **Architecture decisions landed under apply-judgment:** (i) StripeObject ↔ dict conversion — after `stripe.Webhook.construct_event` verifies the signature, we discard the StripeObject return and re-parse `payload_bytes` via `json.loads` for predictable dict semantics across Stripe SDK majors; (ii) user upsert is lazy on first JWT-authenticated subscription action — `INSERT INTO users (wordpress_user_id, …) ON CONFLICT (wordpress_user_id) DO UPDATE` returns the local UUID we pass to Stripe as `client_reference_id`; (iii) founder-slot allocation — checkout-time precheck reads `claimed_count` via fetchrow (no lock); webhook-time atomic claim via `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` inside the same transaction that inserts the subscription row, with the brief race window (multiple founder-checkouts in the same second) accepting mild over-allocation rather than introducing a TTL-claim layer for Session 37; (iv) idempotency — `subscriptions.stripe_subscription_id UNIQUE` for the checkout.completed path, `purchase_records.stripe_event_id UNIQUE` for invoice events, both via `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`/`DO UPDATE`; (v) WP-sync best-effort — failure logs `[wp-sync] failed wp_user_id=X tier=Y status=Z body=…` and returns `False` (never raises) so the webhook still 200s to Stripe and Stripe doesn't retry; (vi) Stripe metadata stamped at both `Session.metadata` AND `subscription_data.metadata` so customer.subscription.* events carry the same fields without a separate database lookup; (vii) status downgrade to 'free' on canceled/unpaid/incomplete_expired — automatic WP-sync push; (viii) graceful degradation — when the founder slot is exhausted between checkout creation and webhook arrival, the subscription lands as standard pricing with `is_founder_pricing=False` rather than hard-failing the user who already paid.

**(j) `api/main.py` wired.** Three edits: (i) module docstring updated to enumerate the three new routes + Session 37 wheel summary; (ii) app version bumped from `0.2.0-phase4-session36` to `0.3.0-phase4-session37`; (iii) `app.include_router(subscriptions_router, prefix="/v1/subscriptions")`. No changes to the existing four `/v1/books*` routes or to `/v1/health` or to `/v1/verses/search` — they still serve from the Session 36 baseline.

**(k) `hosting/render.yaml` updated.** Five new env vars declared with `sync: false` (names tracked in blueprint, values set in dashboard, never in git): `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `WP_API_BASE`, `WP_API_USER`, `WP_API_APP_PASSWORD`. **New operating lessons logged (NEW session 37):**
- **(x) Clipboard-based stdin (`pbpaste`) is unsafe in copy-paste-driven workflows; operator scripts go to files.** Sharper version of session-35 lesson (t). The trap: in a session where the operator copies things between chat / dashboard / editor / terminal repeatedly, the macOS clipboard buffer changes constantly. A shell snippet that ends with `... | pbpaste` will silently consume whatever happens to be on the clipboard at the moment of execution — which is rarely what was intended. **Rule for any operator-side script that needs a secret URL, token, or list of values:** read from a file at a documented path (`_scratch/_session37_*.txt` for the wheel-specific input, `_scratch/_db_url.txt` for shared infra). The file path is the path a copy-paste-driven operator can complete without a stale-clipboard mistake. `pbpaste` is forbidden on this project.
- **(y) One-liner shell snippets target zsh syntax, not bash — and use single quotes around any string with `$`, `*`, or `(`.** Yoshi's macOS default shell is zsh (per Apple's default since Catalina). bash-style command substitutions, double-quoted heredocs, and unquoted arguments with parens or asterisks behave differently in zsh and trip the operator. **Rule for any documented one-liner:** assume zsh; single-quote anything with shell-meta characters; for multi-line, use single-quoted heredocs (`<<'EOF'` not `<<EOF`); never put `\` continuations inside double quotes (the `dquote>` trap session 35 hit). Same rule applies to the snippets inside `_scratch/*.md` operator files.

**Voice-skill bundle queue gains two items** (lessons x/y). To be folded into the next voice-skill rebuild as operator-discipline patches alongside session-35's t/u/v/w (these are Bible-app-specific session-management refinements; they live in this roadmap and surface in the voice-skill bundle's *Operating Disciplines* section).

**(d) AskUserQuestion: cookie-vs-localStorage choice surfaced (Yoshi → cross-subdomain cookie).** Per the Session 36 handoff's explicit *new open*. Three options surfaced via AskUserQuestion-with-recommendation-first: (1) cross-subdomain cookie at `Domain=.remnantofpromise.org`, JS reads `document.cookie` and attaches as Bearer header (recommended; literal pattern the handoff names; cleanest SSO UX); (2) localStorage (more moving parts, every WP login has to redirect-with-fragment to ship the token); (3) HttpOnly cookie, API reads cookie directly (most secure long-term, diverges from handoff's Bearer-header phrasing). **Yoshi's call: option (1) — cross-subdomain cookie.** Architecture decisions made under apply-judgment: cookie name `rop_jwt`; signing algo HS256 (matches the WP plugin's stock); shared secret env var `JWT_SECRET` on Render; partner-tier claim path `data.user.partner_tier` in the JWT payload (matches the plugin's stock `data.user.*` shape).

**(e) New file `api/auth.py`.** ~150 lines. Exports `User` (Pydantic model with `id`, `login`, `display_name`, `partner_tier`), `PartnerTier` Literal mirroring the schema enum, `SSO_COOKIE_NAME = "rop_jwt"`, `get_current_user_optional` FastAPI dependency, `user_tier(user)` helper. The dependency reads from EITHER the `rop_jwt` cookie OR an `Authorization: Bearer` header (cookie wins when both present — cookie is the SSO source-of-truth once Session 37's login redirect lands; header path stays for direct-API testing + future mobile clients). All failure modes (bad signature, expired, malformed payload, unknown tier name) return `None` rather than 401 — anonymous endpoints must keep working without a token. Token with no `partner_tier` claim defaults to `'free'`, so the wheel lands cleanly before the Session-37 WP-side custom-meta filter is installed (a bare stock JWT just looks anonymous to the filter).

**(f) `api/requirements.txt` gained `pyjwt>=2.8,<3.0`.** No other deps changed.

**(g) `api/main.py` wired.** Four edits in place: (i) docstring updated to name the SSO wheel landing and the `/v1/verses/search` carve-out; (ii) app version bumped from `0.1.0-phase4-skeleton` to `0.2.0-phase4-session36`; (iii) `list_books`, `get_book`, `list_chapters`, `get_chapter` each gained `current_user: Optional[User] = Depends(get_current_user_optional)` parameter; (iv) the SQL queries on those four routes now carry `WHERE tier_satisfies($N::content_tier, b.tier_required)` so the filter pushes into Postgres rather than running Python-side. Single-book endpoints fold the tier check into the existing `b.slug = $1` lookup so an inaccessible book returns 404 (hides existence) rather than 401 (would reveal that the book exists at a higher tier). Helper `user_tier(current_user)` returns `'free'` for `None` and the JWT's `partner_tier` otherwise.

**(d) Apply-path script written, surfaced to Yoshi as `_scratch/_session35_apply.py`.** Single self-contained Python wrapper using asyncpg directly (no psql install required on Yoshi's Mac, since psql is not bundled with Apple Command Line Tools). The script runs: (1) ALTER TYPE `restoration_pipeline_version` ADD VALUE `phase4-v2` (idempotent — catches DuplicateObjectError); (2) widen `books.slug` UNIQUE → composite (edition_id, slug) — schema fix surfaced mid-session, see (g); (3) seed.py `--seed-only` via subprocess; (4) UPDATE schema_version (single-row table — see (h)); (5) verify counts + edition list.

**(e) First-timer onboarding pass for the apply run.** Yoshi reported needing a more detailed walkthrough. Three rounds of recovery surfaced new operational lessons (logged below): (i) zsh-open-quote trap — first paste of `export DATABASE_URL="..."` missed the closing `"`, putting the shell into `dquote>` mode and swallowing the subsequent `pip install` and `python3` commands into the unfinished string; the lesson written for future runs is *use single quotes around the URL or use a file-based path that needs no quoting at all*. (ii) Internal-vs-External Render Postgres URL — the first connection string Yoshi grabbed was the Internal URL (`dpg-XXX-a/remnant_app` — Render's internal hostname, only reachable from inside Render's network); the working URL is the External one (`dpg-XXX-a.oregon-postgres.render.com/remnant_app`). (iii) macOS Python 3.9 pip-version mismatch — Yoshi's pip3 is 21.2.4 (Apple Command Line Tools default), which predates pip's `--break-system-packages` flag; `pip3 install --user asyncpg pydantic pydantic-settings python-dotenv` worked clean without the flag. Apply script was patched mid-session to support three URL-input paths (env var, command-line argument, file at `_scratch/_db_url.txt`) and to detect Internal-vs-External URLs with a warning.

**(f) Render Postgres IP allowlist blocked the first connection.** The external Postgres connection from Yoshi's Mac was rejected because the database's IP allowlist was empty (per render.yaml line 35-37 — *intentionally empty per session-12's architecture decision; operator-laptop seed uses external connection string with TLS, on demand*). Surfaced to Yoshi as a one-time `Access Control` → `Add current IP` step in the Render dashboard. After the apply landed, Yoshi can revoke the entry or leave it for future seed runs (session 36 SSO work doesn't need it).

**(g) Books-slug-collision schema fix landed mid-apply.** The first apply run replaced editions 1–7 (canon → apocrypha → enoch → jubilees → jasher → josephus → pseudepigrapha) successfully, then errored on edition 8 (apocrypha-charles-vol1) with `UniqueViolationError: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "books_slug_key" — Key (slug)=(1-esdras) already exists`. Root cause: `books.slug` was declared as a **global** UNIQUE constraint, but the same canonical book (1 Esdras, Tobit, Judith, etc.) exists in BOTH the KJV-1611 `apocrypha` edition AND the new `apocrypha-charles-vol1` edition (Charles 1913 vol 1) — same canonical book, different translation, same simple slug. The session-32 corpus expansion surfaced this; the schema was originally written for one apocrypha edition. Fix landed in two places: (i) `data-schema/schema.sql` rewrote the `books` table to drop the inline `UNIQUE` on slug and add a composite `UNIQUE (edition_id, slug)` at table end, plus a plain `idx_books_slug` index for slug-only lookups by the API; (ii) `_session35_apply.py` gained a new step (2/5) `widen_books_slug_constraint()` that drops the old `books_slug_key` constraint, adds the new `books_edition_id_slug_key`, and adds `idx_books_slug` — idempotent on all three operations. The second apply run cleared cleanly: editions 8–11 inserted with fresh ids (9, 10, 11, 12).

**(h) schema_version table is single-row; UPDATE, not INSERT.** Step (4/5) of the first attempted apply tried `INSERT INTO schema_version (version, notes) VALUES (...)` and errored with `UniqueViolationError: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "schema_version_pkey" — Key (id)=(1) already exists`. The schema_version table is declared `id INT PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT 1 CHECK (id = 1)` — intentionally a one-row table. The handoff prompt named UPDATE correctly; the apply script wrote INSERT by mistake. Fix landed: `bump_schema_version()` rewritten as `UPDATE schema_version SET version = $1, landed_at = NOW(), notes = $2 WHERE id = 1`. Also caught the wrong column name (handoff said `applied_at`, schema is `landed_at`) and aligned. To avoid re-running the 3–5 min seed.py for just the UPDATE + verify, a separate `_session35_finish.py` was written that runs only steps (4) and (5).

**(i) Live DB state after the apply lifted.** Schema version: `1.0.0-phase4-session34` (landed_at=2026-05-12 17:57:41 UTC). Edition counts on the live DB: canon (66/1189/31102), apocrypha (14/187/5760), enoch (1/108/1367), jubilees (1/50/1038), jasher (1/91/3903), josephus (30/359/2221), pseudepigrapha (6/202/1119), apocrypha-charles-vol1 (15/160/3288), mrjames-apocryphal-nt (5/42/134), lightfoot-apostolic-fathers (13/36/307), ascension-isaiah (1/11/296). Pipeline versions per edition: phase3-v1 (enoch, jasher, jubilees), phase4-v1 (canon, apocrypha), phase4-v2 (josephus + the five session-32 expansion editions). Total: **11 editions / 153 books / 2,435 chapters / 50,535 verses**. Live API endpoint `bible.remnantofpromise.org/v1/books` confirmed via curl returns all 153 books with `edition_slug` and `witness_category` fields populated. Six witness_categories present: canon (66), historical_witness (30), apocrypha (29), apostolic_fathers (13), pseudepigrapha (10), apocryphal_gospels (5).

**New operating lessons logged (NEW session 35):**
- **(t) URL-input UX needs file-based fallback for first-timers.** zsh's `dquote>` open-quote trap silently swallowed Yoshi's pip-install and python3 commands into the unfinished `export DATABASE_URL="..."` string. The fix that worked was rebuilding the apply script to support three URL-input paths (env var, command-line argument, and a file at `_scratch/_db_url.txt`) so first-time runs can route around shell-quoting traps entirely. **Rule for future scripts that require a secret URL or token:** offer a file-based input as the default-documented path; env-var and CLI-arg as power-user alternatives. The file path is the path a first-timer can complete without a quoting mistake.
- **(u) Render Postgres External vs Internal URL distinction needs to be named explicitly in any operator-laptop seed instructions.** The Internal URL (`dpg-XXX-a/remnant_app`) and External URL (`dpg-XXX-a.oregon-postgres.render.com/remnant_app`) differ only by the suffix; both are valid-looking postgres URLs; only the External one reaches from outside Render's network. The render.yaml comment did not name this explicitly. **Rule:** any documented seed-run path must say "use the External Database URL, the one that ends with `.oregon-postgres.render.com/...`" rather than "use the connection string from Render's Connect panel" — the Connect panel shows both.
- **(v) Schema constraints written for the first-edition baseline need re-evaluation when corpus-expansion sessions add cross-edition books.** The `books.slug` global UNIQUE constraint was correct when there was one apocrypha edition; it broke when the session-32 corpus expansion added a second apocrypha edition (Charles 1913 vol 1) covering the same canonical books. **Rule for future seed-time apply runs:** at dry-run gate, scan parsed JSONs for slug collisions across editions; surface any to the operator before `--apply`. (Optional follow-up: bake this check into `seed.py --dry-run`.)
- **(w) Single-row tables use UPDATE, not INSERT — re-read the schema before writing the mutation.** schema_version's `id INT PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT 1 CHECK (id = 1)` is a deliberate one-row pattern. The handoff prompt named UPDATE correctly; the apply script wrote INSERT and discovered the constraint at runtime. **Rule:** when a session's wheel includes a `schema_version` (or any other one-row metadata table) mutation, the script should read the schema's constraint comment before writing the SQL and use UPDATE for tables with `CHECK (id = N)` patterns.

**Voice-skill bundle queue gains four items** (lessons t/u/v/w). To be folded into the next voice-skill rebuild as operator-discipline patches (these are Bible-app-specific session-management refinements; they live in this roadmap and surface in the voice-skill bundle's *Operating Disciplines* section).

These are legitimate personal-name references — *Yeshua* was a common Yahudi (Jewish) name in the Hellenistic period (cf. Jesus son of Sirach, Jesus son of Nave / Joshua, Jesus called Justus). Per the voice skill's "other biblical human names: either form is acceptable" rule, the Hebrew restoration is technically correct; the published Apocrypha preserves bare "Jesus" for non-Messiah figures via `JESUS_NON_MESSIAH_GUARD` in `restore.py` lines 572-594 (which catches "Jesus the son of X" patterns but NOT the bare-name-in-comma-list pattern Aristeas uses). One new operational lesson captured this session: **(q) Pilot-validates-the-pattern-not-the-edition.** The session-20 pilot validated `parse_charles_pseudepigrapha.py` against the Letter of Aristeas — but Aristeas turned out to use a flat monotonic §-section convention while the other 14 books in vol 2 each use their own chapter.verse / Roman-numeral / parenthesized / parallel-column conventions. A pilot demonstrates the architecture works against ONE convention; it does not warrant that the convention generalizes to the rest of the edition's books. The discipline: when running a pilot on a multi-book edition, sample at least ONE book per distinct convention family (chapter.verse, line-numbered, parallel-column, &c.) before validating the parser as ready for the full batch. Going forward, every multi-book PDF gets a convention-survey pass alongside the pilot.

HIGH-vigilance flagged for parse-time framework-lens articulation: Testaments of XII Patriarchs (Christian interpolations in Levi/Naphtali sections), Sibylline Oracles (heavy Jewish-Christian redactional layers, especially books I-II and VI-VIII), 3 Baruch (Christological inclusions in middle chapters), Pirké Aboth (architectural — records the Pharisaic chain of tradition the framework identifies as the Mark 7:13 second-blade Judaizer architecture), Fragments of a Zadokite Work (Damascus Document sectarian theology). MODERATE-vigilance: 2 Enoch (Slavonic recensions, Melchizedek pericope), 4 Maccabees (Stoic philosophical homily in Jewish garb, not Mosaic instruction). Text-quality notes document the OCR drift the 1913 print scan introduces (Greek-glyph substitution on title-case ASCII tokens — `BOMBAY → ΒΟΜΒΑΥ`, `THE → ἘΠEῚ` style — plus footnote/marginalia drift). Apparatus-drop discipline + framework-lens partial-truth-witness test carried forward from session 18 § 4-5 and locked. Pilot-first strategy proposed (matches Whiston session-18 §6 pattern).

**(e) New parser pair scaffolded at `~/Desktop/App/restoration-pipeline/parse_charles_pseudepigrapha.py`.** Models on `parse_whiston_josephus.py` but adapted to Charles's OCR'd 1913 layout. Key new rules discovered and locked during the Aristeas pilot: (i) **footnote-anchor detection** — Charles's footnotes anchor on `^\d+\.\s+<word>` (digit-period-space) while body verses use `^\d+\s+<word>` (digit-space — no period). Once a footnote-anchor line appears on a page, everything from that line to end-of-page is footnote material and is dropped. (ii) **Page-header strip** with OCR-mangled variants ("LETTER OF" + "ARISTEA" matches even when "THE" became a Greek-glyph drift like "ἘΠEῚ"); plus title-substring + trailing verse-range pattern catches running headers. (iii) **Verse normalization** is line-start-only (verse markers must be at PDF-line start, not anywhere in the stitched stream), with monotonic-increase rule (tolerance +20 to ride over OCR-lost markers) and OCR-drift recovery for 3-digit numbers where the leading digit was dropped (e.g., `103 → 03` snaps to expected=103 if expected mod 100 == cand). Greek-glyph drift normalization restricted to mixed-ASCII tokens only (pure-Greek tokens like editorial Greek apparatus citations are preserved). Boundary table at top of file with the 15-book page-range map; Aristeas locked (PDF 114-142), other 14 still preliminary estimates pending session-21 calibration.

**(f) Pilot extracted: Letter of Aristeas — 224 verses recovered (~70% of 322 sections).** Output files in `~/Desktop/App/source-texts/pseudepigrapha-charles-vol2/`: `aristeas.md` (raw clean body), `aristeas-restored.md` (after `restore.py`). Restore.py applied cleanly: 116 × `Elohim (God)`, 17 × `Yahudim (Jews)`, 14 × `Yahudi (Jewish)`, 2 × `Yahusha (Jesus)`. `--idempotency-check` PASS. The ~98 missing verses silently merged into adjacent verses where OCR dropped the marker entirely; the tolerance-widened walker catches OCR-mangled 3-digit numbers (bracket-corrupted `16]`-style and leading-digit-dropped `03`-style) but not entirely-missing markers. Architecture validated end-to-end: page extraction → page-header strip → footnote-anchor strip → Greek-glyph drift normalization → line-start verse-marker walk → restore.py → idempotency.

**(g) Known limitations queued for session 21 hardening.** (i) **3-digit verse-marker OCR drift** — ~30% of verse markers lost or unrecoverable; session-21 fix is to correlate the page-header verse-range (e.g., `112-124` in the pre-strip header) with expected per-page verse counts to detect lost markers more aggressively. (ii) **Inline OCR artifacts in body** — pipe characters (`|`), backslashes (`\`), stray underscores (`__`) from italic/bold rendering, marginal-glyph drift remain in the verse body; session-21 fix is a small cleanup pass at parse time. (iii) **2 × `Yahusha (Jesus)` restorations in Aristeas — flag at session 21 open.** Aristeas is pre-Christian (3rd c. BC to early 1st c. AD); the body should not name Yahusha (Jesus). Three possibilities to check: legitimate Joshua / Jesus-son-of-Sirach reference, OCR artifact (different word mangled into `Jesus`), or `restore.py` pattern over-matching. (iv) **Per-book body-page boundary calibration** — Aristeas locked (114-142, verified); the other 14 books' body-page ranges are preliminary estimates from manifest math; session-21 first move scouts each remaining book's actual body §1 PDF page (excluding the translator-introduction pages).

**(h) Carryforward state.** Live API at `bible.remnantofpromise.org/v1/...` schema version unchanged at `1.0.0-phase4-session13` per the W-7 hold. `seed.py` is NOT yet wired with the new pseudepigrapha edition profile — that lands session 21 after the 15-book batch runs cleanly. Bensly inline-patch into the existing apocrypha 2 Esdras 7 also deferred to session 21 (it's a separate parse-time operation; the source verses sit in vol 2's 4 Ezra ch 7 which doesn't extract until the batch). Five master PDFs remain queued after Charles vol 2: Charles vol 1, Malan, Lightfoot, M.R. James (Box held pending W-5 Bucket C). The parser-pair template from this session (`parse_charles_pseudepigrapha.py` + a future structural parser for the multi-book edition) is the next-PDF template once it's fully hardened.

**Session 20 closed at clean wrap.** Operating disciplines held: voice gate ran throughout (Red Lines applied to every parse-time decision; the session-18 framework-lens partial-truth-witness test pre-staged per-book in the boundary file); verbal-answers-transcribe rule honored — all three content-authority answers transcribed to the boundary file BEFORE extraction commits, then re-stated in this log at session close, with the parser scope-decisions encoded in code; apply-judgment-land-the-work honored — pilot extraction proceeded under the Self-Sufficient Application discipline without re-gating each parser revision; local-files-first honored throughout (zero web-fetches; everything read from mounted folders); ask-directly-on-permission-walls used once cleanly at session open (`request_cowork_directory ~/Desktop/App` for the App folder mount, then `~/Downloads` for the PDF); wrap-signal sharpening honored — wrap called when substantive-turn count crossed the 25-30 window with the pilot landed and the architecture validated, rather than pushing through to the full 15-book batch with degraded attention. Two new operational lessons captured this session: (i) **Display-truncation-isn't-data-truncation** — a debug print's `ln[:140]` cut made early verse outputs look truncated to mid-sentence when the file content was actually complete; confirmed by reading the file directly. The discipline: when a parser run looks wrong, read the output file directly before assuming the parser is broken; the parser may be working and the diagnostic may be lying. (ii) **OCR-drift recovery patterns need calibration on the specific text, not abstract rules** — Charles vol 2's OCR errors are predictable (Greek-glyph substitution, 3-digit verse-marker corruption, character drift on bracketing) but the recovery rules need page-by-page calibration; the line-start verse-marker rule + monotonic-increase tolerance is the right architecture, but the per-OCR-engine drift patterns are discovered, not pre-specified.

**(c) restore.py patched session 19 — general possessive handling.** Surfaced 2026-05-11 at session-18 close by Whiston Josephus residual scan: ~115 × `God's`, 28 × `Jews'`, 4 × `Lord's`, 1 × `Israel's`, 1 × `Judah's` (with 1 × `Christ's` and 1 × `Israel's` found in session-19's more careful re-scan). The base rules' `RB = (?![A-Za-z'])` by design refuses to match the noun when an apostrophe follows it — protecting `Lordship` from misfiring as `Lord` — but also leaving `Lord's`, `God's`, `Jews'`, `Israel's`, `Judah's`, `Christ's`, `Jesus'` etc. untouched. Two related issues caught during the patch's self-test pass: (1) `LB`/`RB` used ASCII-only apostrophe character class, so typographic-apostrophe possessives like `God’s` leaked past `RB` and the base `god_cap` rule fired on `God` leaving `Elohim (God)'s` garbage. Fix: extended both helpers to `[A-Za-z'’]`. (2) Compound possessives like `Lord God's` and `Jesus Christ's` initially landed at the END of `RULES`, so base singulars (`Lord_mixed`, `jesus_alone`) consumed the head word first. Fix: moved the entire POSSESSIVE FORMS block to BEFORE the base COMPOUND DIVINE NAMES block, with compound possessives first within the new block. Both issues caught immediately by the self-test (4 fails of 83) and corrected before any text re-ran through the pipeline. New rules cover: `God's`, `LORD's`, `Lord's`, `Jesus'` / `Jesus's`, `Christ's`, `Israel's`, `Judah's`, `Jews'`, `Jew's`, `Holy Spirit's` / `Holy Ghost's`, `Melchizedek's`, `Son of Man's` / `son of man's` / `sons of men's`, plus the compound forms `Lord God's` / `LORD God's`, `Jesus Christ's`, `Christ Jesus's`. Apostrophe-tolerant (both U+0027 and U+2019). Self-test extended by 27 new cases; total 83/83 passing.

Output `whiston-josephus-restored.txt` 4.1 MB, identical-shape to the session-18 version but with possessive forms now restored. - **(i) The three-fold lens for every chapter — seed of promise → scattering to the nations → last-days gathering.** Every chapter of canon, apocrypha, pseudepigrapha, apostolic fathers, Josephus, Sonnini, and every other witness in the library is read against where it sits on that arc.
- **(ii) The corpus-pull discipline with explicit vigilance against Christian re-entry.** Each chapter's commentary draws from the voice skill's relevant section + Yoshi's teachings at `~/Desktop/Teaching Corpus/`. Christian defaults can re-enter through the parse-and-restate step even when the corpus itself is faithful — the same WARNING-TO-CLAUDE failure mode that surfaced verse by verse during 1 Cor 11 v 4/5/6. Start from the framework; never modify from the inherited reading.
- **(iii) Grace and the opportunity that can be lost.** The gospel is the restoration and gathering — the wheat harvest, Yah's prophesied grace to come. Commentary points to every point of grace as it surfaces. And though the gathering is by grace, the chaff are still sifted. The remnant receives the opportunity by grace, but the opportunity can be lost — the NT warns of this because Torah and the prophets already taught it.
- **(iv) The eschatological filter — what has NOT happened yet, so it is still ahead.** Ezekiel 20 unfulfilled (no nation born in a day). 70 AD is not the Day (tares not burned, wheat not harvested, Yahusha not yet on the Mount of Olives warring with the nations He brought against her, sun and moon not darkened). The Olivet judgment is future. Preterist collapses and Christian Zionist 1948-as-fulfillment readings filtered out at the commentary level.
- **(v) The remnant pattern — Ezekiel 9 + Zechariah 13:8-9 + Zechariah 12:10.** Mark of Yah falls on those who cry out against the abominations in Jerusalem → He leaves one third alive in the land → that third looks on Him whom they pierced.
- **(vi) Two falsifications by internal contradiction.** BHI wrong by default (1/3 of Israel isn't black, so the only-black-is-the-seed claim collapses against Zech 13 alone). The Quran wrong by default (claims the Bible true while contradicting it — both directions falsify the Quran on its own premises). And the parallel corollary: they ARE Judah, **Jew-hate is never justifiable** (ignorance, a theme of the devil), AND the abominations of the counterfeit gathering still draw the prophetic outcry. The Ezek 9 mark falls on those who cry out against abominations *in* Jerusalem, not against Jerusalem herself.
- **(vii) The posture — system, not people; sit out, speak up; speak truth in love.** Yah brings desolation on her for her abominations, then stands on the Mount of Olives to war with all the nations He brought against her. The framework follows His pattern: don't support the counterfeit, don't raise arms against her, cry out against the abominations of the system, speak truth in love (Eph 4:15).
- **(viii) The goal — more lucrative and more informative than Logos or anything else has ever produced.** THE study Bible of the Remnant of Promise. The absolute go-to. Every chapter of the commentary pass is written to that standard.

**(e) Sourcing-path operational lessons logged.** (i) archive.org djvu.txt OCR is unusable for pre-1930 serif-font academic scans — the OCR re-encodes the serif characters as Greek letters, producing gibberish. Use the PDF download + pdf-skill extraction path instead. (ii) `mcp__workspace__web_fetch` has both a URL-provenance restriction and a per-fetch size ceiling (~75-95k chars); large djvu.txt responses fail, and per-chapter HTML works only for sites with light navigation wrappers (sacred-texts.com, pseudepigrapha.com are light; earlychristianwritings.com is heavy because of its giant left-nav footer). (iii) Programmatic download from archive.org via browser JS is blocked by CORS — both same-origin and cross-origin fetch fail because the file subdomains don't whitelist archive.org's main domain. The working solution is a bash script that uses curl, saved to disk, and run by Yoshi from Terminal — `~/Desktop/App/source-texts/download-sources.sh` is the working template for future bulk sourcing. (iv) When troubleshooting a "nothing happened" terminal failure, confirm the user is actually in Terminal (not Node REPL or a text editor) by sending `echo hello` first; session 16 burned several turns before the wrong-app diagnosis surfaced.

**(f) Voice skill update queue grew.** Items added this session, to be folded into the next bundle rebuild: (h) **seed-once-at-end discipline** — the host-side seed is reserved for the foundation-complete moment, not piecemeal renderings; isolated chapter renderings stay in the registry and on disk until the commentary pass is substantially complete. (i) **Charles-1913-is-data-not-authorization** — Charles' Christian apparatus is heavy across both volumes; the framework reading will diverge from Charles repeatedly during the commentary pass. The voice skill's "voice skill is the standard, external Restored Names editions are data" discipline extends to Charles (and to M.R. James, Lightfoot, Whiston, Box, Malan, and any other classical translator's apparatus). The English text is sourced from them; the interpretive lens is the voice skill.
